2. Key Technical Concepts:

- LR1121 (Core1121-XF Waveshare): HP PA only (R3=0R, R4=NC), TCXO 1.6V, DIO5/DIO6 for RF switch, setRfSwitchTable() must be called before AND after begin()
- LoRa SF10/BW125/CR4-5: symbol time 8.192ms, preamble 100.4ms, airtime formula n=8+ceil((8·PL+4)/40)·5
- 99-byte wall: at SF10 a 1Hz packet cannot exceed 99 bytes (plain decimal 112B worst-case fails; base-36 90B worst-case passes)
- Base-36: value × scale → integer → uppercase base-36 text; round-trip exact when dp=log10(div); uses double+llround (not float, which loses precision at ±16M for lat/lon)
- I2C bus recovery: 9 manual SCL pulses before Wire.begin(), every boot — all sensors share one physical bus
- BMP585 configuration: OSR_CONFIG=0x61, ODR=0x14, DSP_IIR=0x00; DSP_CONFIG (0x30) NEVER written; single 6-byte burst for T+P
- ICM-20948: bus speed MUST be 100 kHz (400 kHz broke it on this harness); WHO_AM_I must be read with bank 0 forced first; bypass bit for AK09916
- AK09916: bypass bit must be asserted AFTER ICM defaults; ST2 must be included in every 9-byte burst
- SD logging: 128-byte LogRecord (4 per 512B block), pre-allocated contiguous 32MB, sync() every 2000ms
- Flight FSM: FS_BOOT/LAUNCH/EJECT/SEC_EJECT/TOUCHDOWN with FSM_PERSIST_N=4 consecutive samples; FSM_SEC_EJECT_AGL_M=600 (§5.4.vi mandate)
- Silent boot: TX_ENABLED_AT_BOOT=false (§6.1.v)
- Mission clock persistence: missionS() = missionBase + (millis()-bootMs)/1000 across resets (§6.1.ix)
- 90-minute TX auto-off from launch detection (frequency allocation mandate)
- Staged TX power: +10 dBm pad → +14 dBm at FS_LAUNCH
- Antenna: λ/4 at 867 MHz = 86.5 mm; aluminium closer than this detunes the element; glass fibre (εr≈4.4) is RF-transparent
- Post-landing range: two-ray regime, 40 dB/decade — mast-height limited, not power limited
- Schema drift guard: b36SchemaSum()=0x007C must match on both TX (Teensy) and RX (ESP32)

4. Errors and Fixes:

- **Wire.setDefaultTimeout(100) doesn't exist on Teensy 4.1**: That's an i2c_t3 call (Teensy 3.x). Fixed by implementing transaction-level fault counting → Wire.end() + 9 SCL pulses + Wire.begin() after I2C_FAULTS_TO_RECOVER=3 failures.
- **SF/frequency mismatch**: I incorrectly changed to SF9/865MHz/sync 0x25 based on PDR. The frequency allocation document (row 025) confirmed the original firmware values were correct: 867.00MHz/SF10/0x98. Reverted.
- **+22 dBm over-assignment**: The frequency plan assigns +10 dBm pad / +14 dBm flight. Previous firmware was at +22 dBm everywhere. Fixed with staged power via setTxPower().
- **Battery confusion**: Bench ADC reads 7.87V (2S), but CAD has 3 × 21700 cells and PDR declares 3S BMS. My "correction" to 2S GCS limits was wrong and reverted. The bench pack was not the flight pack.
- **Binary format not compliant**: §6.3.i mandates ASCII comma-separated. Binary removed. Base-36 adopted (ASCII, comma-separated, ordered).
- **buildPacket() airtime math error**: I previously claimed SF10 ASCII costs 1190ms using wrong formula (4×8 instead of 4×SF=40). Corrected: SF10 max 99B=985ms.
- **Base-36 float precision**: lroundf(lon * 1e6f) quantises longitude into ~10m steps above 16,777,216 (float mantissa limit). Fixed by using double + llround throughout.
- **TX_OFF bug**: Old code called radio.standby() between listen windows. Now RS_RX_CONT: never standby, receiver always armed, defensive re-arm every 10s.
- **BMP585 blank display**: Vendor library left IIR at coeff 127 (~12s time constant), re-entered Wire, used two separate burst reads (torn reads = ±90m spikes). Fixed by direct register access + single 6-byte burst.

5. Problem Solving:

- Established that the 99-byte wall at SF10 is the binding constraint, not range
- Base-36 solves it: worst-case 90B vs decimal 112B, uplink window opens every 3 cycles not 5
- Python verification suite confirmed codec correctness over full physical range
- Range analysis showed post-landing range is mast-height limited (two-ray regime, 40dB/decade)
- Antenna λ/4 clearance issue identified: aluminium plates within 86.5mm of element detune it; element must be mounted axially outside the stack on glass-fibre standoffs
- b36SchemaSum()=0x007C serves as drift detection — both boards print it at boot

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-03T18:20:57)

Yes — specifically **HFSS**, and one simulation is worth more than all the others combined.

## The one that matters: aluminium plates vs your antenna

This is exactly the unknown I flagged. λ/4 at 867 MHz is 86.5 mm and your vehicle is 150 mm wide, so the aluminium *will* interact with the element — the question is how much, and where the nulls land. HFSS answers that precisely, and it's a sub-wavelength problem (0.43λ × 0.65λ), so it's small and fast.

What you'd get out:
- **Realized gain at 867 MHz for *your* vehicle** — replaces the vendor's unverified 3.58 dBi with a real number that feeds straight back into `ANT_GAIN_CANSAT_DBI` and the link budget
- **3D pattern with the null map** — tells you where to mount the element, and what a tumbling descent actually costs you
- **S11/VSWR at 867** — how far the aluminium detunes it

You can test five mounting positions in a day. That's the thing a bench test can't do cheaply.

## Priority after that

**2. Antenna-to-antenna isolation, 867 MHz TX → GNSS L1 at 1575 MHz.** Classic CanSat killer: a +14 dBm transmitter tens of mm from a receiver listening at −130 dBm. If GPS drops lock every time you transmit, this is why. It also produces the artifact §6.vi mandates — *"teams will be required to present the radio interference mitigation plan and strategy during the PDR stage"* — and you don't have one yet.

**3. Icepak — sealed-enclosure thermal.** You logged 49.6 °C CPU in open air. §5.2.i requires the electronics enclosed, and a Teensy 4.1 at 600 MHz plus the LR1121 PA plus two buck converters in a sealed aluminium tube with no convection will run hotter. Worth knowing before it throttles or the BMP585 reads thermal drift as altitude.

**4. SIwave/Q3D — only once you have a real PCB.** This would finally settle the 400 kHz I²C question with numbers instead of the current "it broke, so we stay at 100 kHz." Simulate 2.2 kΩ pull-ups against real trace capacitance and find out whether 400 kHz is actually available to you.

Maxwell isn't worth it — the N20 is an off-the-shelf motor.

## Three things that will actually block you

**Your Fusion model has 941 bodies. You cannot feed that to HFSS.** You need a separate, deliberately crude EM model: aluminium plates, shell, GFRP rods, antenna element, and plain blocks where the PCBs and battery sit. Every fillet, thread, fastener and connector gets deleted. This is where most student HFSS attempts die — budget more time for geometry prep than for solving.

**You don't have the RadioMaster's internal geometry.** Model an equivalent (sleeve or half-wave dipole tuned to 867), validate it in free space against the vendor's 3.58 dBi first, *then* add the structure. You're measuring the delta the aluminium causes, which is the number you actually need.

**Student licence caps you at 64,000 3D mesh elements** ([HFSS Student Limitations](https://ansyshelp.ansys.com/public//Views/Secured/Electronics/v252/en/Subsystems/HFSS/Content/GettingStarted/HFSSStudentLimitations.htm), [free download](https://www.ansys.com/academic/students/ansys-electronics-desktop-student)). A simplified CanSat at 867 MHz plausibly fits — tight, but doable, and symmetry halves it. At 2.4 GHz the same geometry is ~8× the mesh and won't. **Check whether LPU has a campus licence first** — most Indian engineering universities do, and it removes the limit entirely.

Materials to set: aluminium as finite conductivity (3.8e7 S/m, not PEC), GFRP as ε_r ≈ 4.4 / tan δ ≈ 0.015, printed shell ε_r ≈ 2.7.

## What it won't do

Ansys won't run your firmware, won't model the BMP585 or ICM-20948, and won't validate the base-36 codec. Those stay bench and hardware-in-the-loop problems.

## The honest cost comparison

A **NanoVNA is ~₹3,000 and measures your real antenna on your real vehicle in an afternoon** — including the aluminium detuning, which is the thing you care about. HFSS is roughly a week of geometry work before the first useful plot.

Use both, in this order: NanoVNA to find out *whether* you have a problem, HFSS to find out *where to move the antenna* if you do. If the VNA shows VSWR under 2:1 at 867 with the aluminium installed and the field test closes 2 km, you may not need HFSS at all — except that §6.vi requires you to *present* an interference plan, and an HFSS isolation study is a much stronger PDR artifact than a paragraph of prose.

Want me to add an "EM analysis plan" section to `COMPLIANCE_AUDIT.md` covering the §6.vi requirement, so you have something concrete to point at for CDR?

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-03T18:45:01)

With a full commercial licence (no mesh caps) you can run everything that matters. Here's what's achievable, in priority order for your CanSat:

---

## 1. HFSS — Antenna + Structural EM (highest value)

**What you simulate:** The RadioMaster whip mounted on or near your CanSat body, with aluminium plates, GFRP rods, and the shell present as real materials.

**What you get out:**
- Realized gain pattern at 867 MHz — this replaces the vendor's 3.58 dBi number in your link budget with the actual number for *your* vehicle geometry
- S11/VSWR at 867 MHz — tells you how much the aluminium detunes the antenna and how much power is reflected rather than radiated
- 3D null map — where the pattern dies, critical for a tumbling descent
- Effect of mounting position: axial (outside the stack, on GFRP standoff) vs. radial — you can test 5 positions and see which wins before cutting anything

**Materials you need to assign:** aluminium ε_r = 1, σ = 3.8×10⁷ S/m; GFRP ε_r = 4.4, tan δ = 0.015; PLA shell ε_r = 2.7, tan δ = 0.01; LR1121 module as a PEC block at its footprint size.

---

## 2. HFSS — TX-to-GPS Isolation Study

**What you simulate:** Two ports — the LR1121 antenna at 867 MHz and the L89 GNSS patch at 1575 MHz — with the full vehicle body between them.

**What you get out:**
- S21 (isolation) between the two ports at both frequencies
- Whether +14 dBm TX saturates the GNSS front-end (if isolation < 40 dB with a −130 dBm GNSS sensitivity, you have a problem)
- Exact physical spacing and orientation needed to reach safe isolation

This directly satisfies **§6.vi** — the interference mitigation plan the competition requires you to present at PDR/CDR. An S21 plot from HFSS is a hard deliverable, not a paragraph of prose.

---

## 3. Icepak — Sealed Enclosure Thermal

**What you simulate:** The sealed aluminium tube with Teensy 4.1, LR1121, two buck converters, and the battery — no convection (sealed), only conduction through the structure and radiation to ambient.

**What you get out:**
- Junction temperature of the Teensy at 600 MHz under continuous TX load — you already measured 49.6 °C in open air; enclosed it will be higher
- Hot spots on the PCB
- Whether you need a thermal pad between the Teensy and the shell, or whether the battery becomes the heat sink

The number you care about: Teensy 4.1 throttles at ~85 °C. If Icepak says you're approaching that in a sealed tube at 45 °C ambient (summer launch), that's a design change before you build, not after.

---

## 4. SIwave — I²C Signal Integrity

**What you simulate:** Your PCB with 2.2 kΩ pull-up resistors, real trace geometry, and all four I²C devices (BMP585, ICM-20948, AK09916, DS3231) as load capacitances.

**What you get out:**
- Rise/fall times at 100 kHz vs 400 kHz — this finally gives you a number for whether 400 kHz is physically available on your harness, instead of the current empirical "it broke so we stay at 100 kHz"
- Crosstalk between I²C lines and the SPI1 bus to the LR1121
- Whether your current pull-up value is optimal or needs to change

---

## 5. Q3D Extractor — PCB Parasitics

**What you simulate:** The high-current paths: battery → BMS → buck converters → Teensy, and the RF trace from LR1121 to the SMA connector.

**What you get out:**
- Inductance and resistance of the power distribution network — tells you the voltage droop during TX bursts (LR1121 at +14 dBm pulls ~120 mA for ~1 second per packet)
- Parasitic inductance of the RF feed line — affects match at 867 MHz if the trace is not 50 Ω

---

## Practical order to actually do this

| Week | Simulation | Why first |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | HFSS antenna + aluminium | Drives antenna mounting decision — must be done before you drill holes |
| 1 | HFSS TX/GPS isolation | Required for PDR §6.vi deliverable |
| 2 | Icepak thermal | Drives thermal management before final assembly |
| 3 | SIwave I²C | Nice-to-have, resolves the 400 kHz question |
| 3 | Q3D RF feed | Only if you're doing a custom PCB with an RF trace |

---

## The one thing that will block you most

**Geometry prep, not the solver.** You cannot feed Fusion 360's 941-body assembly directly into HFSS. You need a separate, deliberately dumb EM model:

- Aluminium plates → flat PEC/conductivity shells
- GFRP rods → dielectric cylinders, no threads or end caps
- PCBs → flat FR4 slabs with a copper layer, no components except the antenna element and the SMA pad
- Battery → PEC block at correct size
- Every fastener, fillet, and PCB component → deleted

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-03T19:35:37)

Everything is written. Here's what you have and what came out of the analysis.

## The finding that justifies the whole exercise

I measured your plates in Fusion: **Ø110.00 mm, circumference 345.58 mm.** λ at your assigned 867 MHz is **345.78 mm**.

That's a **0.06% match to a full-wave resonant loop** — and you have five of them stacked 18–28 mm apart, which is 0.05–0.08λ, i.e. tightly coupled to each other.

In the model today they're EPX resin, which is a dielectric and completely harmless. The plan to fabricate them in aluminium turns all five into resonant parasitic elements ringing your antenna. Two more near-resonant conductors are in there too: the PCB ground planes (1.04λ perimeter) and the gyro wheel (0.945λ).

I'm not claiming it will fail. I'm claiming the pattern will not be the vendor's 3.58 dBi doughnut, and the only honest way to know by how much is to solve it. If it's bad, the fix — a 5 mm slot in each rim, or 10 mm off the diameter — is one Fusion edit today and essentially impossible after fabrication.

## Files created

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| [ANSYS_HFSS_GUIDE.md](ANSYS_HFSS_GUIDE.md) | The click-by-click guide. All parameters calculated, not guessed. |
| [ansys/build_cansat_em.py](ansys/build_cansat_em.py) | Builds the entire HFSS model in one run |
| [ansys/slot_the_plates.py](ansys/slot_the_plates.py) | Cuts the rings open, for the comparison run |
| [ansys/add_gnss_port.py](ansys/add_gnss_port.py) | Adds the GNSS antenna for the §6.vi isolation study |

## The most important instruction

**Do not import your Fusion model into HFSS.** It has 269 components, 750+ bodies, a 3280-face hex-lattice shell, duplicate overlapping plates, and the four rods aren't even assembled — they're sitting in a row off to the side at X = 180–300 mm. Importing that is where every "Ansys gave me errors" story comes from.

I already extracted every dimension you need, so the script builds a clean model from primitives in about 20 seconds. **You need no Fusion work at all for simulation #1.**

## Your path

1. Open `D:\Software\Ansys\ANSYS Inc\v261\AnsysEM\ansysedt.exe`
2. Guide §3 — four one-time settings
3. Guide §4.1 — new HFSS design, **Driven Modal**, **units mm** (skipping units is the #1 beginner error — everything becomes 1000× too big)
4. **Tools → Run Script…** → `build_cansat_em.py`
5. Validate, Analyze All — 10–30 min
6. §4.5–4.7 read S11, 3-D pattern, horizon gain
7. §4.8 — copy the design, run `slot_the_plates.py`, solve again, **compare**

Step 7 is the actual deliverable. The difference between the two runs is your decision.

## Two things I calculated so you don't have to run them

**Skin depth in aluminium at 867 MHz = 2.77 µm.** Your 10 mm plates are 3600 skin depths thick, so HFSS must not mesh their interiors — the script sets `Solve Inside = False`. That single setting is what keeps this solvable on a laptop.

**Icepak is unnecessary — skip it.** Steady dissipation ≈ 2.0 W over 0.0886 m² of enclosure gives ΔT ≈ 2.3 K, and your shell is 96% open so it isn't sealed anyway. That's a week saved. Working shown in §8.

## One caveat, stated plainly

I wrote the three scripts against the AEDT COM scripting API but **I have no way to execute them from here** — I can drive Fusion, not Ansys. The geometry maths and the parameters I'm confident in; a scripting API call could still throw. If any line errors, paste me the message and the line number and I'll fix it directly. That's a much shorter loop than hand-entering 40 coordinates.

Also worth knowing before you present this: §9 lists the model's honest limitations. The antenna is a generic λ/4 monopole, not the RadioMaster's undisclosed internal geometry — so absolute gain is approximate, but the *difference* between slotted and unslotted is reliable, and that difference is what you're actually deciding on.

REMEMBER AND IMPROVE IN OUR CODE The initial failure of the Semtech LR1121 radio module, indicated by the -707 error code, was caused by a mismatch between your physical board layout and the Teensy 4.1's default internal silicon routing. While you correctly soldered the Master In Slave Out (MISO) data line to Pin 39, the Teensy's default software configuration automatically assigned the SPI1 receiver channel to Pin 1. Because the microcontroller was actively listening to an unconnected pin, it read floating high static noise instead of the valid acknowledgment signals physically returning from the radio. This static was mathematically interpreted by the RadioLib software as an invalid command status, forcing the system to reject the initialization sequence. By injecting the `SPI1.setMISO(39)` command, you manually reprogrammed the Teensy's internal hardware multiplexer to bridge its listening channel to the correct physical pad, instantly resolving the communication breakdown and allowing the devices to synchronize.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T05:19:01)

Saved to memory, and the code now defends against it recurring.

## One thing worth knowing

Your file already attributed `-707` to a **different** cause — fact #2, `setRfSwitchTable()` being cleared by `begin()`. That was also real, debugged months earlier.

So `-707` has had **two unrelated causes** on this hardware. `RADIOLIB_ERR_SPI_CMD_FAILED` only means "the status byte wasn't a success code" — it says nothing about why. I documented both rather than overwriting one with the other, because next time someone hits `-707` they need to know it's ambiguous, not chase the wrong one for a day.

## What I added

**`rfErrName()`** — boot now prints `code=-707 — SPI_CMD_FAILED (status byte was not OK)` instead of a bare integer.

**`diagnoseSpi()`** in [Radio.cpp](vritra_fc/Radio.cpp) — runs only when `begin()` has already failed. It resets the chip, issues a raw `GetVersion` (opcode `0x0101`), and classifies what comes back on MISO:

| Raw bytes | Verdict |
|---|---|
| all `0xFF` | **The exact bug you hit.** MCU listening to a floating pin — `setMISO()` lost, or the joint is open |
| all `0x00` | MISO shorted low, or module unpowered |
| BUSY stuck high | Chip not booting — check RESET polarity and 3V3 |
| mixed | **Chip is talking.** Wiring is fine — look at the RF switch table, `TCXO_V`, or the frequency/power args |

It also dumps the live pin numbers, so a wrong `Config.h` is visible in the same breath.

That last row is the valuable one: it tells you when to *stop* suspecting wiring. That's the distinction that cost you the day.

**Gateway** — added the same error naming to [vritra_gs.ino](vritra_gs/vritra_gs.ino), with a note that this specific failure **cannot occur there**. ESP32's `SPI.begin(SCK, MISO, MOSI, CS)` takes pins as explicit arguments, so there's no default pin to be left behind. If the gateway reports `-707`, it's the switch table or a genuinely dead module.

## The load-bearing detail I made explicit

`setMISO`/`setMOSI`/`setSCK` **must all precede `SPI1.begin()`** — afterwards the peripheral is already latched to the old pins and the calls are silently ineffective. That ordering is now commented in the code as load-bearing, because it's the kind of thing that looks like harmless reordering during a cleanup.

## One honest caveat

`diagnoseSpi()` uses a 2 ms busy-wait for the reset pulse, which your project otherwise forbids outside I²C bus recovery. I allowed it on the grounds that it only ever runs on a boot path that has already failed and is about to `return false` — there's no cadence left to protect. It's stated as such in the comment so it doesn't read as an accidental violation. Say the word if you'd rather it be a state machine.

I haven't compiled this — no Teensy toolchain here. The `RADIOLIB_ERR_*` constants and the LR1121 `GetVersion` framing are standard, but a build is the check.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T06:14:34)

I found three concrete bugs and fixed them. I did **not** get to everything you asked — details at the end.

## 1. GNSS — almost certainly your main problem

**The modular rewrite dropped `addMemoryForRead()`.** Your old monolithic `vritra_tx.ino` had it on line 789. `Sensors.cpp` never called it.

Teensy's default UART RX buffer is **64 bytes = 66 ms at 9600 baud**. One 1 Hz NMEA burst (GGA+RMC+GSA+GSV+GIGSV) is 400–700 bytes and takes ~600 ms to arrive. Every `loop()` stall longer than 66 ms — an SD `sync()`, a 944 ms SF10 transmit slot — overruns it. Bytes vanish mid-sentence, the checksum fails, `nmeaLine()` discards the line.

**The receiver works fine and the antenna is fine. The bytes never survive the trip.** That matches your symptom exactly: it worked before (7–11 satellites), stopped working after the rewrite. Now buffered at 1024 bytes (~1.06 s of slack).

## 2. GNSS — `CMD_GPS_RESET` bricked the receiver until power cycle

`gnssHardReset()` pulled `RESET_N` low and jumped to `IS_GNSS_PORT`. But **only `IS_RTC` ever raised it again.** So any GPS reset command held the L89 in reset permanently.

Both paths now route through `IS_GNSS_DETECT`, the single place `RESET_N` is released, with a proper 50 ms pulse. If you sent a GPS reset during testing, that alone would explain total silence afterward.

## 3. IMU — DEVICE_RESET added, done the way you described

Your diagnosis was right. Two details I'd flag:

**It runs *before* the probe, not after.** A wedged ICM fails `WHO_AM_I`, so a reset placed after a successful probe never runs in exactly the case that needs it. It's now issued blind and unconditionally — one wasted write when healthy, a rescue when not.

**It only resets `0x69`, deliberately.** `ICM_ADDR_B` is `0x68`, which on your harness is the **DS3231** — and DS3231 register `0x06` is the *year* register. A blind `0x80` there writes an invalid BCD year and corrupts your RTC date. Your ICM straps AD0 high, so `0x69` alone is sufficient and safe. Commented so nobody "helpfully" adds `0x68` later.

## Why your 3D model doesn't move — diagnosed, not fixed

This isn't a bug, it's the 99-byte wall. Your packet sends **one optional field per second on a 19-field rotation**. `GX`, `GY`, `GZ`, `MX`, `MY`, `MZ` are all in that rotation — so **each gyro axis updates once every 19 seconds.** If the GCS model is driven by them, it is genuinely frozen.

`ACC_X/Y/Z` and `SPIN_RATE` *are* in the core packet at 1 Hz. Roll and pitch are derivable from accel alone at full rate; only yaw needs the magnetometer.

Two fixes, your call:
- **Drive the model from core `ACC_X/Y/Z`** — free, works today, 1 Hz
- **`OPT_PER_FRAME` 1→2** — I checked the budget: worst case 90 B + 6 B = 96 B, still under the 99 B wall. Halves refresh to ~9.5 s.

## What I did not do

Being straight with you, because you asked for a lot:

- **Antenna research** (web/YouTube/Reddit/GitHub) — not started. It's a substantial separate task and the GNSS bugs were blocking.
- **Base-36 re-verification** — not re-run. It passed 120,000 round-trip tests earlier and schema sum `0x007C` is unchanged, so I have no reason to suspect it, but I didn't re-check.
- **Auto-reconnect after link loss** — not implemented. The right fix is persisting the TX-enabled flag to EEPROM alongside the mission clock, so a brownout doesn't silently re-mute you. §6.1.ix arguably *requires* this ("system state shall be maintained"). Needs a change in `vritra_fc.ino`.
- **The 5-minute radio acquisition** — I can't diagnose it from here. The `diagnoseSpi()` output added last turn will tell you whether it's SPI, the RF switch table, or genuinely RF.

**One thing to confirm before you flash:** your new pin map moved GNSS to Serial2 (pins 7/8) and `RESET_N` to pin 6. If the harness isn't physically rewired yet, GNSS stays dead regardless of these fixes.

Want me to take the auto-reconnect next, or the antenna research?

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T06:22:46)

Verification complete. The headline first.

## Your firmware has not compiled at all

```
Config.h:109: error: expected constructor, destructor, or type conversion before 'AUTO'
Config.h:111: error: invalid digit '8' in octal constant
Sensors.cpp:359: error: 'GNSS_PORT' was not declared in this scope
```

A **GNSS port auto-detection** feature was added to `Config.h` but only half-finished. Line 108 closed the comment block and line 109 continued as if it hadn't, so prose was being parsed as C++. Worse, `GNSS_PORT` was split into `GNSS_PORT_A`/`_B` and `Sensors.cpp` was never updated.

**Every module failed. Whatever is on your Teensy is an older build.** That explains a lot of what you were seeing — you've been debugging hardware against firmware that couldn't be flashed.

I fixed the comment and finished the feature, because it directly solves your problem.

## GNSS port auto-detection — now actually implemented

At boot the firmware opens **both** Serial2 and Serial1, listens on both for up to 3 s, and locks onto whichever delivers a `$`. The boot log now says:

```
// [GNSS] locked to Serial2
```

or, if neither answers:

```
// [GNSS] NO DATA ON EITHER PORT after autodetect.
// [GNSS]   Not a wiring-port problem — both were listened to.
// [GNSS]   Check 3V3, RESET_N (pin 6) idle HIGH, and TX/RX not swapped.
```

That second message is the valuable one: it **rules the port wiring out**, leaving only module, power, or antenna. Previously "no GNSS" meant four indistinguishable problems.

One detail that would have bitten you: Serial1's RX is **pin 0, which your new map assigns to BMP585 INT**. The loser port is now explicitly `end()`ed and pin 0 restored to a plain input.

## Verification results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `Sensors.cpp` / `Radio.cpp` / `DataLogger.cpp` / `Motor.cpp` | **CLEAN**, `-Wall`, zero warnings |
| `vritra_fc.ino` | **CLEAN** |
| `Base36.h` drift guard | **byte-identical**, md5 `3961c215…` both copies |
| Base-36 codec | **ALL PASS** — 120k round-trips, schema `0x007C` |
| Packet sizes | typical 73 B / 780 ms · worst 90 B / 944 ms — both under the 99 B wall |
| `delay()` in flight path | none (comments only) |
| `delayMicroseconds` | only the 2 documented sites |
| `volatile` on ISR-shared state | `irqFlag`, `cmdPending_` ✓ |
| Critical section on shared queue | `noInterrupts()`/`interrupts()` ✓ |
| ISR length | `onDio9()` is one line ✓ |
| Watchdog | armed, `__has_include` guarded ✓ |

Built with your real toolchain: `arm-none-eabi-g++ 15.2.1`, Teensy core 1.62.0, cortex-m7 hard-float.

## The GNSS buffer number

```
1024 bytes × 10 bits ÷ 9600 baud = 1.067 s of buffering
worst TX slot                     = 0.944 s
margin                            = 123 ms   (and TX is IRQ-driven, not blocking)

previous 64-byte default          = 66.7 ms  ← any SD sync() overran it
```

RAM cost: 2 KB for both buffers on a 1 MB part.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T14:07:29)

Flight firmware compiles clean. Here's everything, in your order.

## 1. Review of changes so far

| Change | Status |
|---|---|
| Pin remap (LR1121 CS/BUSY/RST/IRQ, GNSS→Serial2, RTC SQW→22) | Applied, compiles |
| `Config.h` broken comment block | **Was blocking every build** — fixed |
| GNSS port auto-detection | Was half-finished; now implemented |
| `addMemoryForRead` 64→1024 B | Applied |
| `gnssHardReset()` leaving RESET_N low forever | Fixed |
| ICM `DEVICE_RESET` (blind, pre-probe, 0x69 only) | Applied |
| `rfErrName()` + `diagnoseSpi()` | Applied |
| DS3231 SQW `INPUT_PULLUP` | Applied |
| Base36 codec | ALL PASS, both copies md5-identical |

## 2. Datasheets — what matters

**The file you sent is the Waveshare wiki, not the Semtech datasheet.** The real one is linked on its last page: `files.waveshare.com/wiki/Core1121/LR1121_H2_DS_v2_0.pdf`. Send me that and I'll go through it properly.

Three things from the wiki that change our documents:

**Your connector is IPEX-4 (MHF4), not u.FL.** `Config.h` §5b says "the module is u.FL" and recommends an "IPEX1 (u.FL) version" antenna. **That is wrong and would mean ordering a connector that doesn't mate.** IPEX-4 is physically smaller and incompatible.

**RX current is 9.2 mA @ 125 kHz.** This vindicates the permanent-receive design — staying armed costs almost nothing. TX at 22 dBm is 115 mA; at our +14 dBm it's far less.

**Vendor sensitivity is −127 dBm at SF7**, not SF10. Our −132 dBm at SF10 is a reasonable conservative extrapolation (~+8 dB for three SF steps), but it's ours, not the vendor's.

**Features we're not using that are worth it:** `lr1121_spectral_scan` (RSSI heatmap — scan 865–868 before launch and pick main vs backup on evidence, not guesswork) and `lr1121_tx_cw` (continuous carrier for antenna testing without a VNA — directly relevant since you can't do NanoVNA).

### L89 — four findings, one serious

**Max tracked acceleration is 4 G.** Your ejection shock is ~30 G. The module will likely lose lock at deployment and need to reacquire. Reacquisition is <1.5 s, so it's survivable — but plan for a GNSS gap at ejection rather than being surprised by it.

**Default configuration is GPS+Galileo+IRNSS.** Our `PMTK353,1,1,1,0,1` enables GPS+GLONASS+Galileo+BeiDou. PMTK353 has no IRNSS field — so we're adding two constellations while possibly disturbing the IRNSS setup that's the entire reason for choosing an L89.

**9600 baud is marginal.** Five constellations at 1 Hz produce ~700 B/s of NMEA against a 960 B/s ceiling — 73% utilisation, and it rises with satellite count. Combined with the old 64-byte buffer this was almost certainly fatal. Worth trimming GSV rate via PMTK314.

**It has embedded patch and chip antennas.** No external antenna needed — but that patch sits inside your aluminium plate stack. This is the same problem as the HFSS study, and it may be a real contributor to poor GNSS.

**TTFF cold start is <32 s without AGPS.** You waited far longer than that. This is a fault, not acquisition time.

**QCOM_V1.6** is Quectel's serial terminal. Connect the L89 to a USB-TTL adapter and run it — that answers "is the module alive?" in two minutes, with the Teensy entirely out of the picture.

## 3. IMU data — fixed, and the cause was not the IMU

Gyro axes were in the 19-field rotation, so **each axis refreshed once every 19 seconds**. An attitude display fed at 1/19 Hz doesn't look slow, it looks broken. Nothing was wrong with the sensor or the link.

`GX/GY/GZ` now ship in **every packet**. Byte arithmetic against the 99 B wall:

```
worst case:  core 81 B + gyro 15 B = 96 B  ≤ 99  ✓
typical:     core 64 B + gyro 12 B = 76 B, optional still fits at 84 B
```

The optional block's existing fit guard gives adaptive degradation for free: on a full-deflection packet the graded core plus attitude always goes out, and only the non-graded rotating value is dropped.

**Barometer was already correct** — `ALT`, `PRES`, `TEMP` have always been in the 1 Hz core. If it's blank on your GCS, that's a display or sensor-init problem, not the packet.

Gateway updated to match: gyro at wire positions 18–20, and the optional value moved 19→22.

## Two things you must know

**The wire format changed. Flash both boards together.** An old gateway against new firmware will mis-parse every field after FLAGS.

**I could not compile the ESP32 gateway** — no Xtensa toolchain run yet. The flight side is verified clean; the gateway edits are not. That's the one gap in "no errors" and I'd rather say so than let you find out on the pad.

Next most valuable: the real Semtech datasheet, or the ESP32 build.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T14:17:12)

Compiles clean. I found something significant in the datasheet.

## The finding: your radio has never been calibrated for 867 MHz

LR1121 User Manual §2.1.3, verbatim:

> During the startup sequence, the device firmware calibrates the low and high frequency RC oscillators, the PLL, the ADC, and the image rejection of the mixer **at 915MHz**.

> Image calibration is necessary if there is a **frequency change > 10MHz**, or a temperature change > 10 °C.

> By default, the image calibration is made in the band **902 - 928MHz**.

**You transmit at 867.00 MHz — that is 35 to 61 MHz outside the calibrated band, three to six times past the stated threshold.**

It compounds. The datasheet adds that when a TCXO replaces the crystal — your case — *"all start-up (POR) calibrations are skipped. The host processor should program the TCXO configuration and re-launch the calibrations."* RadioLib's `begin()` programs the TCXO but does not re-launch calibrations.

**This does not break the link. It quietly weakens it.** Degraded image rejection means the receiver is several dB less sensitive than your budget assumes — so acquisition is slow and marginal and the link "takes minutes to come up" rather than failing outright. That is precisely the behaviour you described.

Added `calibrateImageRejection(863.0f, 870.0f)` to **both** boards, covering main and backup channels.

## Second free gain: RxBoosted was off

Datasheet Table 3-8 quotes **every** sub-GHz LoRa sensitivity figure with `RxBoosted = 1`:

| BW 125 kHz | Sensitivity |
|---|---|
| SF7 | −127 dBm |
| SF12 | −141 dBm |
| **SF10 (interpolated)** | **≈ −135 dBm** |

Your `Config.h` claims −132 dBm, which is safely conservative — **but only if boosted gain is enabled, and it is not the default.** Now enabled on both boards. This matters more on the ground station, which is the end doing the hard work of hearing a +14 dBm transmitter 2 km away.

## Other confirmations from the documents

**TCXO 1.6 V is now datasheet-backed**, not just empirical — Table 3-13 gives 1.6 V typical. The comment in `Config.h` warning against re-deriving it from the schematic can stand with a real citation behind it.

**Schematic confirms** the PE4259 switch on RFSW0_V1/RFSW1_V2 (our DIO5/DIO6 table is right), R3=0R / R4=NC (HP path only, as documented), 32 MHz TCXO, and an RClamp1561PWQ TVS on the antenna. There's also a 32.768 kHz crystal we don't use — that's for LoRaWAN timing.

**Frequency-offset tolerance at SF10 is ±25% of BW** = ±31 kHz = 36 ppm at 867 MHz. Your TCXO is far tighter, so no concern.

## What I skipped, deliberately

**The two LoRaWAN packages are not applicable.** Your frequency allocation mandates raw LoRa point-to-point with a fixed sync word — you are not joining a network, there is no gateway or join server. The backend-interfaces spec describes server-to-server APIs that don't exist in your architecture. Reading them would not change a line of code.

## Still open

- **ESP32 gateway is still not compiled** — I've now made three edits to it (gyro decode, index shift, calibration) all unverified. This is the real risk left.
- Antenna research, auto-reconnect persistence.
- I have not opened `Core1121_XF_Demo.zip` — worth checking whether the vendor's own init sequence does anything else we're missing.

**Flash both boards together** — the wire format changed last turn and hasn't been flown yet.

If you want one thing next, it's the ESP32 build. Three unverified edits sitting on the receiving end of your only link is the weakest point in the system right now.

see gps is still not connection see this one of the old code i am sending in that it worked a liitle better see how it was able to do that only for gps. that old code has old connection so be carefull "/*
 * VRITRA  ·  2026-INSPACe-CAN-7USAT-025
 * Teensy 4.1 + Semtech LR1121 (Core1121-XF)  —  FLIGHT TRANSMITTER
 *
 * SPI:  CS=10  CLK=13  MOSI=11  MISO=12  RST=16  BUSY=14  IRQ=15
 * GPS:  Serial1  RX=0  TX=1  @9600 Bd  (Quectel L89)
 * I2C:  Wire  SDA=18  SCL=19
 *
 * RF:   867.00 MHz · SF10 · BW125 kHz · CR4/5 · Sync 0x98 · Pre 8
 *       HP PA @ 14 dBm · TCXO 1.6 V · explicit header · CRC-2
 *
 * Cycle: transmit one frame (~1 s air-time) → if pktCount % 4 == 0,
 *        open 400 ms RX window for uplink commands.
 *        Average downlink rate ≈ 0.91 Hz. Meets ≥ 1 Hz mandate for
 *        core data; command window is narrow so the ESP32 must queue
 *        and send immediately after receiving each downlink packet.
 *
 * Libraries (install via Library Manager):
 *   RadioLib, TinyGPSPlus, Adafruit_BMP3XX, Adafruit_LSM6DSO32,
 *   SparkFun ICM-20948 (ICM_20948), INA226_WE, BH1750,
 *   Adafruit AS7343, Sensirion I2C SCD4x, RTClib
 *
 * Sensors marked [STUB] return 0 until you integrate their library.
 */

#include <RadioLib.h>
#include <SPI.h>
#include <Wire.h>
#include <TinyGPSPlus.h>
#include <Adafruit_BMP585.h>
#include <Adafruit_LSM6DSO32.h>
#include <ICM_20948.h>
#include <INA226_WE.h>
#include <BH1750.h>
#include <Adafruit_AS7343.h>
#include <SensirionI2CScd4x.h>
#include <RTClib.h>

// ─── RF pin map ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
#define PIN_CS    10
#define PIN_IRQ   15
#define PIN_RST   16
#define PIN_BUSY  14

// ─── RF parameters — Team 025 allocation, do NOT change SF/BW/Sync ─────────
#define FREQ_MAIN   867.00f
#define FREQ_BACKUP 865.75f
#define LORA_SF     10
#define LORA_BW     125.0f
#define LORA_CR     5
#define LORA_SYNC   0x98
#define LORA_PRE    8
#define TX_POWER    14          // dBm, always HP PA
#define TCXO_V      1.6f        // Core1121-XF on-board TCXO

// ─── Timing ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
#define RX_WINDOW_MS   400UL    // uplink listen window after TX
#define CMD_EVERY      4        // open RX window every N packets

// ─── Flight states (match GCS: BOOT=0 IDLE=1 LAUNCH=2 EJECT=3 SEC_EJECT=4) ─
enum FlightState : uint8_t {
    FS_BOOT = 0, FS_IDLE = 1, FS_LAUNCH = 2,
    FS_EJECT = 3, FS_SEC_EJECT = 4, FS_TOUCHDOWN = 5
};

// ─── RF switch table — Core1121-XF (DIO5=V1, DIO6=V2) ──────────────────────
static const uint32_t SW_PINS[] = {
    RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
    RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t SW_TABLE[] = {
    { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },   // LP path (unused, keep for table)
    { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },   // HP path ← used by setOutputPower(x, true)
    { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
    END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};

// ─── Objects ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
LR1121            radio(new Module(PIN_CS, PIN_IRQ, PIN_RST, PIN_BUSY, SPI));
TinyGPSPlus       gps;
Adafruit_BMP3XX   bmp;
Adafruit_LSM6DSO32 lsm;
ICM_20948_I2C     icm;
INA226_WE         ina(0x40);
BH1750            bh1750;
Adafruit_AS7343   as7343;
SensirionI2CScd4x scd41;
RTC_DS3231        rtc;

// ─── Flight state ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
volatile bool pktFlag  = false;
uint32_t      pktCount = 0;
FlightState   state    = FS_BOOT;
uint8_t       frame    = 0;         // 0=A 1=B 2=C 3=D
bool          txEnabled  = true;
bool          armed      = false;
bool          beaconOn   = false;
bool          useBackup  = false;
float         padAlt_m   = 0.0f;    // ground reference, set by CALIBRATE

// ─── GPS extras ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
uint8_t  navicSats  = 0;
uint8_t  fixQuality = 0;
float    hdop_val   = 0.0f;
float    vdop_val   = 0.0f;
float    geoidSep   = 0.0f;
uint8_t  gnssMask   = 0;    // constellation bitmask
String   nmeaBuf    = "";

// ─── Sensor cache ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
struct {
    float pres_pa  = 101325.0f;
    float temp_c   = 25.0f;
    float bus_v    = 12.0f;
    float bus_i_ma = 0.0f;
    // LSM6DSO32 (core IMU, ±32g, units m/s²; gyro Z in deg/s for SPIN_RATE)
    float lsm_ax = 0, lsm_ay = 0, lsm_az = 0;
    float spin_dps = 0;
    // ICM-20948 (9-DOF)
    float icm_ax = 0, icm_ay = 0, icm_az = 0;  // g
    float gx = 0, gy = 0, gz = 0;              // deg/s
    float mx = 0, my = 0, mz = 0;              // µT
    // high-g (same LSM, converted to g)
    float ahx = 0, ahy = 0, ahz = 0;
    // env
    float hum_pct = 0, hdc_t = 0, hdc_rh = 0;
    uint16_t co2 = 400, tvoc = 0, eco2 = 0;
    uint8_t  aqi = 1;
    // PM [STUB — integrate BMV080 SDK]
    float pm1 = 0, pm25 = 0, pm10 = 0;
    // light
    float lux = 0;
    float uv  = 0;                  // mW/cm²  [ML8511 on A0]
    float o3  = 0;                  // ppb     [MQ131 on A1 STUB]
    // lightning [STUB — integrate AS3935]
    float lgt_dist = 0, lgt_int = 0;
    bool  lgt_flag = false;
    // AS7343 spectral (0=405, 1=425, 2=450, 3=475, 4=515, 5=550,
    //                  6=555, 7=600, 8=640, 9=690, 10=745, 11=855,
    //                  12=CLR, 13=FLK)
    float spec[14] = {};
    // servo / sys
    float servo_v  = 0, servo_i = 0;
    int   srv[4]   = {};            // degrees
    float sd_pct   = 0, flash_pct = 0;
    // RTC
    char utc[12] = {};
} S;

// ─── ISA: pressure → altitude (m) ────────────────────────────────────────────
static float p2alt(float p_pa) {
    // Troposphere: T0=288.15 K, L=0.0065 K/m, R=287.05, g=9.80665
    return (288.15f / 0.0065f) *
           (1.0f - powf(p_pa / 101325.0f, 287.05f * 0.0065f / 9.80665f));
}

// ─── Base-36 encoder (handles negatives with '-' prefix) ─────────────────────
static String b36(long v) {
    if (v == 0) return "0";
    bool neg = v < 0;
    if (neg) v = -v;
    static const char D[] = "0123456789ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ";
    char buf[14]; int i = 13; buf[i] = '\0';
    while (v > 0) { buf[--i] = D[v % 36]; v /= 36; }
    if (neg) buf[--i] = '-';
    return String(buf + i);
}
// Scaled shorthands
static inline String bi(float v)  { return b36((long)roundf(v)); }
static inline String b1(float v)  { return b36((long)roundf(v *    10.0f)); }
static inline String b2(float v)  { return b36((long)roundf(v *   100.0f)); }
static inline String b3(float v)  { return b36((long)roundf(v *  1000.0f)); }
static inline String b4(float v)  { return b36((long)roundf(v * 10000.0f)); }

// ─── Radio IRQ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
void onPacket() { pktFlag = true; }

// ─── Radio init ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
static bool radioInit() {
    SPI.begin();
    radio.setRfSwitchTable(SW_PINS, SW_TABLE);
    float f = useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN;
    int16_t st = radio.begin(f, LORA_BW, LORA_SF, LORA_CR,
                             LORA_SYNC, TX_POWER, LORA_PRE, TCXO_V);
    if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
        Serial.printf("[RF] begin FAILED code=%d\n", st);
        return false;
    }
    radio.setRfSwitchTable(SW_PINS, SW_TABLE); // must set again after begin
    radio.setCRC(2);
    radio.setRegulatorDCDC();
    radio.setSyncWord(LORA_SYNC);
    radio.setOutputPower(TX_POWER, true);       // true = force HP PA

    LR11x0VersionInfo_t vi;
    if (radio.getVersionInfo(&vi) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE)
        Serial.printf("[RF] HW=0x%02X DEV=0x%02X FW=%d.%d  %.2f MHz SF%d BW%.0fk OK\n",
            vi.hardware, vi.device, vi.fwMajor, vi.fwMinor, f, LORA_SF, LORA_BW);
    return true;
}

// ─── NavIC / constellation NMEA parser ────────────────────────────────────────
static void parseNmea(const String& line) {
    if (line.startsWith("$GIGSV,")) {   // NavIC sats in view
        // $GIGSV,<msg_cnt>,<msg_num>,<total_sats>,...
        int c1 = line.indexOf(',');
        int c2 = line.indexOf(',', c1+1);
        int c3 = line.indexOf(',', c2+1);
        int c4 = line.indexOf(',', c3+1);
        if (c4 > c3) navicSats = (uint8_t)line.substring(c3+1, c4).toInt();
    }
    // Track constellation bitmask (GPS=1 GLONASS=2 GALILEO=4 BEIDOU=8 QZSS=16 NAVIC=32)
    if (line.startsWith("$GPGSA")) gnssMask |= 1;
    if (line.startsWith("$GLGSA")) gnssMask |= 2;
    if (line.startsWith("$GAGSA")) gnssMask |= 4;
    if (line.startsWith("$GBGSA")) gnssMask |= 8;
    if (line.startsWith("$GQGSA")) gnssMask |= 16;
    if (line.startsWith("$GIGSA")) gnssMask |= 32;
    // VDOP from $GPGSA / $GNGSA  (field 17 before checksum)
    if ((line.startsWith("$GPGSA") || line.startsWith("$GNGSA")) && line.length() > 30) {
        int fc = 0; int pos = 1;
        while (fc < 17 && pos < (int)line.length()) {
            if (line[pos] == ',') fc++;
            pos++;
        }
        int end = line.indexOf(',', pos);
        if (end < 0) end = line.indexOf('*', pos);
        if (end > pos) vdop_val = line.substring(pos, end).toFloat();
    }
    // GGA fix quality and geoid from TinyGPSPlus; parse here for geoid only
    if ((line.startsWith("$GPGGA") || line.startsWith("$GNGGA")) && line.length() > 40) {
        // Field 11 = geoid separation
        int fc = 0; int pos = 1;
        while (fc < 11 && pos < (int)line.length()) {
            if (line[pos] == ',') fc++;
            pos++;
        }
        int end2 = line.indexOf(',', pos);
        if (end2 > pos) geoidSep = line.substring(pos, end2).toFloat();
    }
}

// ─── GPS feed (call every loop iteration) ─────────────────────────────────────
static void pollGPS() {
    while (Serial1.available()) {
        char c = (char)Serial1.read();
        gps.encode(c);
        if (c == '\r') {
            parseNmea(nmeaBuf);
            nmeaBuf = "";
        } else if (c != '\n') {
            nmeaBuf += c;
            if (nmeaBuf.length() > 120) nmeaBuf = "";
        }
    }
}

// ─── Sensor reads ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
static void readSensors() {
    // BMP388
    if (bmp.performReading()) {
        S.pres_pa = bmp.pressure;
        S.temp_c  = bmp.temperature;
    }

    // LSM6DSO32 (core IMU: m/s²; gyro: rad/s → deg/s for spin)
    {
        sensors_event_t a, g, t;
        lsm.getEvent(&a, &g, &t);
        S.lsm_ax   = a.acceleration.x;
        S.lsm_ay   = a.acceleration.y;
        S.lsm_az   = a.acceleration.z;
        S.spin_dps = g.gyro.z * (180.0f / PI);  // rad/s → deg/s
        // high-g in g units
        S.ahx = a.acceleration.x / 9.80665f;
        S.ahy = a.acceleration.y / 9.80665f;
        S.ahz = a.acceleration.z / 9.80665f;
    }

    // INA226
    S.bus_v    = ina.getBusVoltage_V();
    S.bus_i_ma = ina.getCurrent_mA();

    // ICM-20948
    if (icm.dataReady()) {
        icm.getAGMT();
        // accX() returns mg; gyrX() returns deg/s; magX() returns µT
        S.icm_ax = icm.accX() / 1000.0f;
        S.icm_ay = icm.accY() / 1000.0f;
        S.icm_az = icm.accZ() / 1000.0f;
        S.gx     = icm.gyrX();
        S.gy     = icm.gyrY();
        S.gz     = icm.gyrZ();
        S.mx     = icm.magX();
        S.my     = icm.magY();
        S.mz     = icm.magZ();
    }

    // BH1750 lux
    S.lux = bh1750.readLightLevel();

    // SCD41 (CO2, temp, humidity) — non-blocking
    {
        bool ready = false;
        scd41.getDataReadyFlag(ready);
        if (ready) {
            uint16_t co2raw; float t2, h2;
            if (scd41.readMeasurement(co2raw, t2, h2) == 0) {
                S.co2    = co2raw;
                S.hdc_t  = t2;
                S.hdc_rh = h2;
                S.hum_pct = h2;
            }
        }
    }

    // ML8511 UV (analog A0): ~0.1 V per mW/cm², 1.0 V = 0 mW/cm²
    {
        float v = analogRead(A0) * 3.3f / 1023.0f;
        S.uv = max(0.0f, (v - 1.0f) / 0.1f);
    }

    // AS7343 spectral (non-blocking read — takes ~10 ms)
    {
        uint16_t raw[10];
        if (as7343.readAllChannels(raw)) {
            S.spec[0]  = raw[0];   // 405
            S.spec[1]  = raw[1];   // 425
            S.spec[2]  = raw[2];   // 450
            S.spec[3]  = raw[3];   // 475
            S.spec[4]  = raw[4];   // 515
            S.spec[5]  = raw[5];   // 550
            S.spec[6]  = raw[6];   // 555
            S.spec[7]  = raw[7];   // 600
            S.spec[8]  = raw[8];   // 640
            S.spec[9]  = raw[9];   // 690
            // 745 and 855 require separate reads on AS7343
        }
    }

    // RTC UTC string
    {
        DateTime now = rtc.now();
        snprintf(S.utc, sizeof(S.utc), "%02d%02d%02d",
                 now.hour(), now.minute(), now.second());
    }

    // [STUB] BMV080 PM — integrate Bosch BMV080 SDK here
    // S.pm1 = ...; S.pm25 = ...; S.pm10 = ...;

    // [STUB] AS3935 lightning — integrate library here
    // S.lgt_dist = ...; S.lgt_int = ...; S.lgt_flag = ...;

    // [STUB] ENS160 AQI — integrate library here
    // S.tvoc = ...; S.eco2 = ...; S.aqi = ...;

    // [STUB] MQ131 ozone (analog A1)
    // S.o3 = ...;
}

// ─── Build one downlink CSV packet ────────────────────────────────────────────
static String buildPacket() {
    // Derived core values
    float baro  = p2alt(S.pres_pa);
    float agl   = baro - padAlt_m;
    long  t_s   = millis() / 1000UL;
    long  gnss_t = gps.time.isValid()
                 ? (long)gps.time.hour()*3600 + gps.time.minute()*60 + gps.time.second()
                 : 0;
    float lat    = gps.location.isValid() ? gps.location.lat() : 0.0f;
    float lon    = gps.location.isValid() ? gps.location.lng() : 0.0f;
    float gpsalt = gps.altitude.isValid() ? gps.altitude.meters() : 0.0f;
    long  sats   = gps.satellites.isValid() ? (long)gps.satellites.value() : 0;
    float gspeed = gps.speed.isValid()     ? gps.speed.mps() : 0.0f;
    hdop_val     = gps.hdop.isValid()      ? gps.hdop.hdop() : 0.0f;
    fixQuality   = gps.location.isValid()  ? 1 : 0;
    float soc    = constrain((S.bus_v - 9.0f) / (12.6f - 9.0f) * 100.0f, 0.0f, 100.0f);

    // Core (fields f[0]-f[18], every frame)
    String p = "025";
    p += ','; p += b36(t_s);
    p += ','; p += b36(pktCount);
    p += ','; p += b1(agl);
    p += ','; p += bi(S.pres_pa);
    p += ','; p += b1(S.temp_c);
    p += ','; p += b2(S.bus_v);
    p += ','; p += b36(gnss_t);
    p += ','; p += b4(lat);
    p += ','; p += b4(lon);
    p += ','; p += b1(gpsalt);
    p += ','; p += b36(sats);
    p += ','; p += b2(S.lsm_ax);
    p += ','; p += b2(S.lsm_ay);
    p += ','; p += b2(S.lsm_az);
    p += ','; p += b1(S.spin_dps);
    p += ','; p += b36((long)state);
    p += ','; p += b36(navicSats);
    p += ','; p += (char)('A' + frame);  // sentinel

    // Frame-specific optional fields
    switch (frame) {
    case 0: // Frame A — power · environment · PM · air quality · lightning
        p += ','; p += bi(S.bus_i_ma);     // f[19] BUS_I_MA (mA)
        p += ','; p += b1(soc);            // f[20] SOC_PCT * 10
        p += ','; p += b2(gspeed);         // f[21] GND_SPEED * 100
        p += ','; p += b1(S.hum_pct);      // f[22] HUM_PCT * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.pm1);          // f[23] PM1 * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.pm25);         // f[24] PM25 * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.pm10);         // f[25] PM10 * 10
        p += ','; p += bi(S.lux);          // f[26] LUX (int lx)
        p += ','; p += b2(S.uv);           // f[27] UV * 100
        p += ','; p += b36(S.co2);         // f[28] CO2 (ppm)
        p += ','; p += b36(S.tvoc);        // f[29] TVOC (ppb)
        p += ','; p += b36(S.eco2);        // f[30] ECO2 (ppm)
        p += ','; p += b36(S.aqi);         // f[31] AQI (1-5)
        p += ','; p += b1(S.hdc_t);        // f[32] HDC_T * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.hdc_rh);       // f[33] HDC_RH * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.lgt_dist);     // f[34] LGT_DIST * 10
        break;

    case 1: // Frame B — ICM-20948 9-DOF · high-g · lightning extra
        p += ','; p += b3(S.icm_ax);       // f[19] AX * 1000 (g)
        p += ','; p += b3(S.icm_ay);       // f[20] AY * 1000
        p += ','; p += b3(S.icm_az);       // f[21] AZ * 1000
        p += ','; p += b1(S.gx);           // f[22] GX * 10 (deg/s)
        p += ','; p += b1(S.gy);           // f[23] GY * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.gz);           // f[24] GZ * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.mx);           // f[25] MX * 10 (µT)
        p += ','; p += b1(S.my);           // f[26] MY * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.mz);           // f[27] MZ * 10
        p += ','; p += b3(S.ahx);          // f[28] AHX * 1000 (high-g)
        p += ','; p += b3(S.ahy);          // f[29] AHY * 1000
        p += ','; p += b3(S.ahz);          // f[30] AHZ * 1000
        p += ','; p += b1(S.lgt_int);      // f[31] LGT_INT * 10
        p += ','; p += b36(S.lgt_flag ? 1 : 0); // f[32] LGT_FLAG
        p += ','; p += bi(S.o3);           // f[33] O3 (ppb)
        break;

    case 2: // Frame C — AS7343 spectral (14 channels)
        for (int i = 0; i < 13; i++) {     // f[19]-f[31] S_* * 100
            p += ','; p += b2(S.spec[i]);
        }
        p += ','; p += b1(S.spec[13]);     // f[32] S_FLK * 10 (Hz)
        break;

    case 3: // Frame D — servos · system · GNSS quality · UTC
        p += ','; p += b2(S.servo_v);      // f[19] SERVO_V * 100
        p += ','; p += bi(S.servo_i);      // f[20] SERVO_I_MA
        for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) {      // f[21]-f[24] SRV*_DEG
            p += ','; p += b36(S.srv[i]);
        }
        p += ','; p += b36(0);             // f[25] MCU_ACTIVE (primary=0)
        p += ','; p += b36(1);             // f[26] WDOG_OK
        p += ','; p += b1(S.sd_pct);       // f[27] SD_PCT * 10
        p += ','; p += b1(S.flash_pct);    // f[28] FLASH_PCT * 10
        p += ','; p += b36(beaconOn ? 1:0);// f[29] BEACON_ON
        p += ','; p += b36(1);             // f[30] CAM1_OK (1 if fitted)
        p += ','; p += b36(1);             // f[31] CAM2_OK
        p += ','; p += b2(hdop_val);       // f[32] HDOP * 100
        p += ','; p += b2(vdop_val);       // f[33] VDOP * 100
        p += ','; p += b36(fixQuality);    // f[34] FIX_QUALITY
        p += ','; p += b1(geoidSep);       // f[35] GEOID_SEP * 10
        p += ','; p += b36(gnssMask);      // f[36] GNSS_SYS bitmask
        if (S.utc[0]) { p += ','; p += S.utc; }  // f[37] UTC (optional)
        break;
    }
    return p;
}

// ─── Execute an uplink command ────────────────────────────────────────────────
static void execCmd(const String& cmd) {
    Serial.print(F("[CMD] ")); Serial.println(cmd);
    if      (cmd == "TX_ON")        txEnabled = true;
    else if (cmd == "TX_OFF")       txEnabled = false;
    else if (cmd == "ARM")          armed = true;
    else if (cmd == "DISARM")       armed = false;
    else if (cmd == "BEACON_ON")    beaconOn = true;
    else if (cmd == "BEACON_OFF")   beaconOn = false;
    else if (cmd == "FORCE_SEC_EJ") {
        if (state == FS_EJECT) state = FS_SEC_EJECT;
    }
    else if (cmd == "CALIBRATE") {
        padAlt_m = p2alt(S.pres_pa);
        Serial.printf("[CAL] pad=%.1fm  P=%.0fPa\n", padAlt_m, S.pres_pa);
    }
    else if (cmd == "FAILOVER") {
        useBackup = !useBackup;
        radioInit();
    }
    else if (cmd == "RESET") {
        SCB_AIRCR = 0x05FA0004;   // Teensy soft reset
    }
}

// ─── Parse an over-the-air uplink packet ─────────────────────────────────────
static void parseUplink(const String& raw) {
    // Format: "025,CMD,<COMMAND>"
    int c1 = raw.indexOf(',');
    int c2 = raw.indexOf(',', c1 + 1);
    if (c1 < 0 || c2 < 0) return;
    if (raw.substring(0, c1) != "025") return;
    if (raw.substring(c1 + 1, c2) != "CMD") return;
    String cmd = raw.substring(c2 + 1);
    cmd.trim();
    execCmd(cmd);
}

// ─── Optional RX listen window (checks for uplink commands) ──────────────────
static void listenForCommand() {
    pktFlag = false;
    radio.setPacketReceivedAction(onPacket);
    radio.startReceive();

    uint32_t t0 = millis();
    while (millis() - t0 < RX_WINDOW_MS) {
        pollGPS();
        if (pktFlag) {
            pktFlag = false;
            String raw;
            if (radio.readData(raw) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE && raw.length())
                parseUplink(raw);
            break;
        }
    }
    radio.standby();
}

// ─── Full TX + optional RX cycle ─────────────────────────────────────────────
static void txCycle() {
    if (!txEnabled) {
        // TX silent: listen continuously so commands still arrive
        listenForCommand();
        return;
    }

    pktCount++;
    String pkt = buildPacket();
    int16_t st = radio.transmit(pkt);

    Serial.printf("[TX] #%lu F%c %dB %s\n",
        pktCount, (char)('A' + frame), pkt.length(),
        st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE ? "OK" : "FAIL");

    frame = (frame + 1) & 3;

    // Open RX window every CMD_EVERY packets (or always in late flight)
    if ((pktCount % CMD_EVERY == 0) || state >= FS_EJECT)
        listenForCommand();
}

// ─── Launch / state detection ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
static void updateState() {
    float az_g = fabsf(S.lsm_az / 9.80665f);

    if (state == FS_IDLE && armed && az_g > 3.0f)
        state = FS_LAUNCH;

    // Detect apogee: two consecutive packets showing altitude drop
    static float prev_agl = 0;
    float agl = p2alt(S.pres_pa) - padAlt_m;
    if (state == FS_LAUNCH && agl < prev_agl - 2.0f)
        state = FS_EJECT;
    prev_agl = agl;

    // Touchdown: low altitude + low vertical motion (rough heuristic)
    if (state == FS_SEC_EJECT && agl < 10.0f && az_g < 1.05f)
        state = FS_TOUCHDOWN;
}

// ─── setup ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
void setup() {
    Serial.begin(115200);
    delay(500);
    Serial.println(F("\n[VRITRA] 2026-INSPACe-CAN-7USAT-025  Teensy 4.1 TX"));
    Serial.println(F("         867.00 MHz  SF10  BW125  CR4/5  Sync 0x98"));

    Wire.begin();
    Serial1.begin(9600);   // Quectel L89

    // Radio
    while (!radioInit()) { Serial.println(F("[RF] retrying in 2 s...")); delay(2000); }

    // BMP388
    if (!bmp.begin_I2C())
        Serial.println(F("[BMP] NOT FOUND — check I2C wiring"));
    else {
        bmp.setOutputDataRate(BMP3_ODR_12_5_HZ);
        bmp.setOversampling(BMP3_OVERSAMPLING_8X, BMP3_OVERSAMPLING_4X);
    }

    // LSM6DSO32
    if (!lsm.begin_I2C())
        Serial.println(F("[LSM] NOT FOUND"));
    else
        lsm.setAccelRange(LSM6DSO32_ACCEL_RANGE_32_G);

    // ICM-20948 (AD0 pin high → addr 0x69)
    icm.begin(Wire, 1);
    if (icm.status != ICM_20948_Stat_Ok)
        Serial.println(F("[ICM] NOT FOUND"));

    // INA226
    ina.init();
    ina.setAverage(AVERAGE_4);
    ina.setConversionTime(CONV_TIME_1100);

    // BH1750
    bh1750.begin(BH1750::CONTINUOUS_HIGH_RES_MODE);

    // SCD41
    scd41.begin(Wire);
    scd41.stopPeriodicMeasurement();
    delay(500);
    scd41.startPeriodicMeasurement();

    // AS7343
    if (!as7343.begin())
        Serial.println(F("[AS7343] NOT FOUND"));
    else {
        as7343.setATIME(29);
        as7343.setASTEP(599);
        as7343.setGain(AS7343_GAIN_256X);
    }

    // RTC
    if (!rtc.begin())
        Serial.println(F("[RTC] NOT FOUND"));

    // Warm up and calibrate ground reference
    delay(500);
    if (bmp.performReading()) {
        S.pres_pa = bmp.pressure;
        padAlt_m  = p2alt(S.pres_pa);
        Serial.printf("[CAL] initial pad alt = %.1f m\n", padAlt_m);
    }

    state = FS_IDLE;
    Serial.printf("[VRITRA] Ready.  Freq=%.2f MHz  Pad=%.1f m\n",
                  useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN, padAlt_m);
}

// ─── loop ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
void loop() {
    pollGPS();
    readSensors();
    updateState();
    txCycle();

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T14:31:05)

Stop — look at your own telemetry. **`GNSS_SYS=5.00`**.

That's the constellation bitmask, and 5 = binary 101 = **GPS + Galileo**. Our parser only sets those bits when it successfully parses `$GPGSA` and `$GAGSA` sentences.

**Your L89 is alive, correctly wired, and streaming valid NMEA that the Teensy is parsing.** The port auto-detect worked. This was never a wiring or UART problem.

And 5 is exactly what the L89 datasheet lists as *"Default Configuration: GPS+Galileo+IRNSS"* — which means **our PMTK config block is being ignored or rejected.** The module is running factory defaults. That's why the old code, which sent no configuration at all, behaved better.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T14:34:04)

All clean. Here's what your telemetry actually proves.

## Your GNSS is not broken

`GNSS_SYS=5` is the finding. That bitmask is set **only** when the parser validates `$GPGSA` and `$GAGSA` sentences — checksums and all. 5 = GPS + Galileo.

So: the module is powered, wired correctly, on the right UART, streaming NMEA, and the Teensy is parsing it successfully. **Everything between the antenna and the packet works.**

And 5 is exactly what the L89 datasheet calls the *factory default* — GPS+Galileo+IRNSS. Our `PMTK353` asks for GPS+GLONASS+Galileo+BeiDou, which would be mask 15. **The module is ignoring our configuration block entirely.**

That matches your old code perfectly: it sent no configuration at all — just `Serial1.begin(9600)` — and reached 7–11 satellites on this same hardware.

## What I changed

`GNSS_SEND_CONFIG` is now `false`. The L89 boots at factory defaults. Two reasons beyond your empirical evidence:

**`PMTK353` cannot express IRNSS.** There is no NavIC field in that command. NavIC is the entire reason you chose an L89, and the factory default already enables it. We were overwriting a good config with one that can't represent what we want.

**Four constellations is ~700 B/s of NMEA against a 960 B/s ceiling at 9600 baud** — 73% utilisation, rising with satellite count. Past 100% the receiver drops sentences on its own.

`PMTK886,2` (aviation dynamic model) is also actively wrong on a bench — it widens the tracking loops for a receiver that isn't moving.

The block is retained and can be re-enabled **one line at a time** once you have a confirmed outdoor fix, so any regression is attributable to a specific command.

## Also fixed

**The `snprintf` truncation warning was real.** `utc[24]` against a format that can emit 26 bytes — `utcYear` and friends are `uint8_t`, so the compiler is right that a corrupt RTC read would silently truncate the timestamp in your flight log. Now 32.

**One `-Wextra` warning in our code** — mixed enum/integer in the `loadState()` conditional. Fixed with a cast; the bound check that protects against corrupt EEPROM is unchanged.

Everything else `-Wextra` reported is Teensy core and EEPROM library noise, not actionable.

All five translation units compile clean under `-Wall`.

## What your log says is working

Radio is healthy: RSSI −68 to −70 dBm, SNR 13–15 dB, PER falling steadily to 7.5% as the window fills. `FW=1.1` confirms the stock transceiver firmware. Uplink works — both `TX_ON` and `CALIBRATE` were acknowledged and executed.

**Gyro is now in every packet** — `GX/GY/GZ` on every line, and packets are 57–60 B, well under the 99 B wall with room for the rotating optional. That fix landed.

Barometer is live: 96996 Pa, 31.3 °C, altitude tracking 0.0/0.1 m.

## The honest next step

**Take it outside.** Cold start is <32 s with clear sky per the datasheet. You are indoors, and the L89's embedded patch antenna is inside an aluminium plate stack — the same shielding problem the HFSS study exists to quantify.

If it still won't lock outdoors with factory defaults, the next test is `QCOM_V1.6` with the L89 on a USB-TTL adapter, Teensy entirely out of the picture. That separates "module can't see sky" from "module is faulty" in about two minutes.

Still unverified: the ESP32 gateway build. It now has four of my edits on it.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T14:43:37)

Your photos show the answer. Look at BODY AXES: ROLL −13.40, PITCH 38.60, TILT 40.52 — those track the physical tilt of the board in your hand correctly. But every one is flagged **STALE**, and `GX/GY/GZ` sat at exactly `0` in the log until the GCS declared them **FROZEN**.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T14:44:09)

Confirmed — that's the bug, and it's in one line.

`GYRO_DEADBAND_DPS = 12.0f` is applied directly to the **telemetry** `gx/gy/gz`. Hand-rotating a board for a demo is roughly 10–40 °/s, so a 12 °/s deadband swallows most of it and emits exactly `0`. The GCS correctly calls that FROZEN.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T14:45:20)

Clean. Your IMU was never broken — the firmware was erasing its output before it reached the packet.

## What was happening

```c
d_.gx = (fabsf(cgx) < GYRO_DEADBAND_DPS) ? 0.0f : cgx;   // 12.0 dps
```

That deadband was applied to the **telemetry** axes. A deliberate demonstration rotation by hand is roughly 10–40 °/s, so nearly everything you did to the board fell under the threshold and went out as a hard `0.0`. The GCS then correctly reported `GX/GY/GZ` as FROZEN and the attitude display as STALE.

Roll and pitch kept working because they come from the accelerometer — which is exactly why your photos show TILT 40.52 tracking the real angle of the board in your hand while the gyro sat dead.

## The fix, and the principle behind it

**Raw gyro now goes out raw** — bias-corrected only, no deadband.

The deadband's original justification was real: gyro noise leaking into `SPIN_RATE` corrupted earlier flight logs. But `SPIN_RATE` is what the **flight state machine acts on**, and a false non-zero there can trigger a real state transition. That still has its deadband.

Raw telemetry is different. A noisy value there costs nothing — the GCS can smooth for display, and the unfiltered stream is what you actually want on the SD card for post-flight review.

> Filter where a wrong value causes a wrong **decision**. Never where it only causes a noisy **display**.

## Attacked the noise at the source too

`GYRO_CONFIG_1` was `0x1F` — DLPFCFG 3, a 51.2 Hz bandwidth. Now `0x27` — DLPFCFG 4, 23.9 Hz.

Gyro noise scales with √bandwidth, so this cuts your measured 4.3–11.2 °/s noise floor by about **1.5×**. It's also the correct anti-alias corner for the 50 Hz sensor tick (Nyquist 25 Hz). Better than hiding noise behind a threshold.

I deliberately left full scale at **±2000 °/s**. A narrower range would be quieter, but ejection shock can spin the vehicle past 1000 °/s, and clipping the one measurement the mission exists to capture isn't a trade worth making.

## For your live presentation

Flash this and the model will track your hand in real time. You should see `GX/GY/GZ` moving continuously with small non-zero values even at rest — **that is correct behaviour**, not a fault. A gyro reading exactly zero is a gyro that isn't telling you anything.

Two things that will still show red, and neither is a firmware bug:

**SATELLITES 0** — you're indoors. Nothing in software fixes that. If the demo needs a fix, the receiver has to see sky.

**BUS_V 8.29 V / SOC 0%** — that's the 2S bench pack against 3S limits (`BATT_CELLS 3`, empty 9.00 V). Expected on the bench, and it'll clear on the flight pack. If you want it green for the demo, that's a one-line change — say the word and I'll make it switchable rather than hard-coded.

Also worth knowing: RSSI improved to −44 dBm in these photos from −68 earlier. Same room, so that's the antenna orientation, not the code.

## Your gyro fix worked — look at your own log

```
PKT #01456   GX=-19.60°/s  GY=-37.40°/s  GZ=1.10°/s
PKT #01457   GX=-32.40°/s  GY=57.90°/s   GZ=-61.90°/s
PKT #01472   GX=-10.40°/s  GY=139.6°/s   GZ=11.80°/s
```

Live, large, tracking every movement. The IMU has been fine.

## Why the model still didn't move

The GCS was reading the **wrong accelerometer fields**.

```python
core_nonzero = (ax_o == 0.0 and ay_o == 0.0 and az_o == 0.0 and ...)
if core_nonzero and core_acc:      # only uses live core if AX/AY/AZ are EXACTLY zero
```

`AX/AY/AZ` live in the 19-field rotating slot, so they always hold a stale-but-nonzero value — the condition was never true. Your log shows it plainly: `AX=-0.006g AY=-0.057g AZ=0.983g` repeats for ~19 seconds while `ACC_X` swings from `-8.14` to `+5.94 m/s²` in the same packets.

**It was driving the 3D model from data up to 19 seconds old, with live data sitting unused in every packet.**

Same cause for `NO HEADING`: yaw needed `MX`, `MY`, `MZ` — all three in the rotation, so they're essentially never in one packet together.

## What I built instead

Deriving attitude on the ground is the wrong architecture. The GCS sees 1 Hz; the Teensy sees 50 Hz. A single accel sample per second shows a static tilt and nothing else — and any sample taken *during* motion has linear acceleration mixed in that can't be separated from gravity.

**A 50 Hz complementary filter now runs on the flight computer** and transmits `ROLL/PITCH/YAW` in every packet:

- Gyro is high-passed (smooth, no lag), gravity is low-passed (absolute, cancels drift), crossing over at ~1 s
- Gravity is only trusted when **|a| is between 0.75 and 1.25 g** — under launch or ejection the accelerometer measures thrust, not down, so blending it would tilt the estimate toward the acceleration vector
- Costs 15 B worst case; packets go 57–70 B → ~85 B, under the 99 B wall

The GCS now prefers these and falls back to derivation for older firmware.

## Honest limits

**Yaw drifts.** It's gyro-integrated — relative rotation, correct for the model, *not* a compass bearing. Absolute heading needs hard-iron magnetometer calibration, which hasn't been run on this vehicle. `HEADING` stays the magnetic field; I didn't relabel it as something it isn't.

## Flash all three together

The wire format changed and the CSV is now 87 columns. Teensy, ESP32, and restart the GCS — a mismatch silently misaligns every column.

## Two things I have to be straight about

**The ESP32 gateway still has not been compiled.** It now carries six of my edits including the N_FIELDS change. That's the biggest remaining risk in the system and I can't clear it without the Xtensa toolchain.

**GNSS: I have not solved it, and I should stop claiming progress I haven't made.** What the data tells us is narrow but real — `GNSS_SYS` went `5 → 1 → 0` across your log. The module is talking and constellation bits are *dropping out*, which is a receiver losing what little it had rather than a dead link. Beyond that I'm guessing, and guessing has cost you time.

The test that ends it: **L89 on a USB-TTL adapter into QCOM_V1.6, Teensy disconnected entirely.** Raw NMEA on screen in two minutes tells you whether the module acquires at all. If it does, the fault is ours. If it doesn't, no firmware change will help and the module or its antenna is the problem.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T15:45:23)

All clean. The bug was in the ground station, not the IMU.

## What was wrong

The renderer's own comments admitted it:

```python
y, z = y * cp - z * sp, ...   # "pitch (about X)"   ← about X is ROLL
x, z = x * cr + z * sr, ...   # "roll  (about Y)"   ← about Y is PITCH
```

**Roll and pitch were fed into each other's rotation.** Tilt the CanSat one way, the model moves the other. That's exactly your screenshot: PITCH 84.40° when you rotated about a different axis.

Second bug in the same three lines: **yaw was applied first**, to the raw body point. The standard aerospace intrinsic Z-Y-X sequence is `R = Rz(yaw)·Ry(pitch)·Rx(roll)`, so on a point the rotations must go roll → pitch → yaw. Applying yaw first made heading changes *tumble* the body instead of spinning it about its own axis.

Both fixed. The firmware was computing correct angles the whole time.

## Sign controls added

The remaining variable is which way each axis points, and that depends on how the ICM-20948 breakout is physically soldered — no amount of maths can infer that. So it's now three constants in `Config.h`:

```c
#define ATT_SIGN_ROLL   (+1.0f)
#define ATT_SIGN_PITCH  (+1.0f)
#define ATT_SIGN_YAW    (+1.0f)
```

**30-second bench test**, watching the BODY AXES panel:

| Step | Do this | Expect | If wrong |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Stand upright, nose up | ROLL ~0, PITCH ~0, TILT ~0 | — |
| 2 | Tilt nose **forward** 45° | PITCH goes **positive** | flip `ATT_SIGN_PITCH` |
| 3 | Upright, then tilt top **right** 45° | ROLL goes **positive** | flip `ATT_SIGN_ROLL` |
| 4 | Upright, rotate **clockwise from above** 90° | YAW **increases** ~90 | flip `ATT_SIGN_YAW` |

Change only the sign, never the magnitude.

**One diagnostic worth knowing:** if an axis moves the right *way* but the wrong *axis* responds — nose-up moving ROLL instead of PITCH — that's not a sign problem. It means the breakout is mounted rotated 90° about the long axis, and the fix is mechanical (or an axis swap in `icmRead()`), not a sign flip. Tell me if you see that and I'll do the swap.

The signs are applied to the filter **output** only. Internal integration stays in one frame, so flipping an axis for display can't feed back and destabilise the filter.

## Flash order

Teensy firmware **and** restart the GCS — the fix spans both.

Then GNSS, and I'll write up the full failure analysis of why it never worked, as you asked.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T15:59:27)

Both changes in, compiles clean.

## 1. Left/right mirror fixed

```c
#define ATT_SIGN_ROLL   (-1.0f)
#define ATT_SIGN_PITCH  (+1.0f)
#define ATT_SIGN_YAW    (-1.0f)
```

I flipped **both** lateral axes, not just one. Roll tilts sideways and yaw turns — both move the vehicle left/right, and you reported the whole lateral response mirrored. That pattern is a handedness mismatch between how the breakout is mounted and the renderer's right-handed frame, not two independent errors. Pitch stays positive since you never said front/back was wrong.

**If flipping these two now breaks front/back**, that's a different fault — the breakout is mounted *rotated* rather than mirrored, and the fix is an axis swap in `icmRead()`, not a sign. Tell me and I'll do it.

## 2. Boots at 0,0,0, shows movement from there

Three seconds after the filter starts, the vehicle's current pose is captured as a datum and subtracted from everything published afterwards. Wherever the CanSat is sitting becomes 0/0/0, and every angle after that is movement relative to it — live at 1 Hz.

You'll see this on the serial console when it happens:

```
// [ATT] datum captured: R-2.4 P1.1 Y183.6 -> 0,0,0
```

**Why 3 seconds and not instantly at boot:** at the first pass the filter has no history — roll and pitch come almost entirely from one accelerometer sample and yaw is arbitrary. Zeroing then would bake that noise into the reference permanently. Three seconds is about 3× the filter's 1 s cross-over, long enough for gravity to pull roll and pitch onto true vertical first.

**`CMD_CALIBRATE` now re-zeros it**, so you can zero the model on the pad once the vehicle is standing in its real launch orientation rather than however it booted.

The yaw subtraction wraps through ±180° before display, so a datum near 360° against a reading near 0° reads as a small movement, not a 360° jump.

## One thing this costs, stated plainly

Roll and pitch are no longer absolute angles from vertical — if the vehicle boots tilted, "0" means *that tilt*, not level.

The absolute measurement isn't lost. **`TILT` in the GCS is still computed from raw `ACC_Z`** and remains a true angle from vertical. It just moved to the field that's actually defined to carry it. So you get relative movement on the model and absolute tilt on the gauge, which is the right split.

Flash the Teensy and restart the GCS. If any single axis is still backwards after this, name which one and it's a one-character change.
