**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T16:07:08)

Written to [IMU_RESEARCH_BRIEF.md](IMU_RESEARCH_BRIEF.md) — copy everything below the horizontal rule into your research tool.

## Why I stopped guessing

I owe you a straight answer about the last three attempts. I was flipping signs based on your description of the symptom, and that's not engineering — it's search. Three sign constants give eight combinations, and if the real fault isn't a sign at all, none of them work. There are three candidate causes I should have separated before touching anything:

**A left-handed frame (determinant −1).** If the sensor-to-vehicle mapping is a *reflection* rather than a rotation, the display is mirrored and no combination of sign flips fixes it — flipping one axis just moves the mirror to a different plane. That matches your symptom exactly: fix left/right, break something else.

**Gimbal lock.** Your log showed `PITCH 84.40°, TILT 88.21°`. Tait-Bryan Euler angles are singular at pitch ±90°. Approaching it, roll and yaw stop being independent and trade off against each other unpredictably. A vertical cylinder that gets laid on its side passes straight through this. If that's the cause, the fix is quaternions, not signs.

**The AK09916 axis mismatch.** The magnetometer inside the ICM-20948 package reportedly does *not* share the accel/gyro axis frame — it needs a fixed transform. I've seen this referenced but haven't verified it against the datasheet, so the brief asks for primary-source confirmation rather than asserting it.

The brief asks the researcher to **rank these against everything you've observed and name the single measurement that discriminates between them.** That's the question worth answering.

## What's in the brief

Your exact configuration — register values, measured noise floors, bias numbers, filter coefficients — so nobody has to guess at your setup. Seven numbered research questions. Nineteen sources split into primary datasheets, standards and textbooks, papers, reference implementations, and community threads flagged as needing verification.

The important one is **§4: measure the rotation matrix empirically** with a six-position static test instead of guessing signs. That gives you a 3×3 matrix you can check — orthonormal, determinant +1. If the determinant comes out −1, that's your bug, proven rather than suspected.

It also constrains the deliverable to a bench procedure needing **no turntable and no rate table** — a flat desk, a spirit level, and your hands.

## Meanwhile

The current firmware still has `ATT_SIGN_ROLL = -1` and `ATT_SIGN_YAW = -1` from my last guess. When the research comes back, revert those to `+1` before applying whatever it says — otherwise you'll be stacking a real fix on top of a wrong one and it won't converge either.

Send me what comes back and I'll implement it properly. If it points at quaternions I'll replace the Euler filter with Mahony or Madgwick, which also removes the gimbal-lock problem permanently.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T16:27:48)

Everything compiles, schema aligned at 88 both sides, and the worst case now fits: **97 B, 985 ms — inside the 1 Hz budget with 2 bytes to spare.**

## The report gave you a definitive answer, and I had it wrong

Your mirrored display was **two compounding faults**, and sign-flipping could never have fixed either:

**Gimbal lock.** Z-Y-X Euler angles are singular at pitch ±90°. The inverse Euler matrix contains `tan(θ)` and `sec(θ)`, which diverge there; roll and yaw become collinear and the system loses a degree of freedom, producing sudden 180° phase flips. Your `PITCH 84.4°` / `TILT 88.2°` readings were the vehicle sitting on that singularity. A CanSat descends vertically and tumbles through zenith — **Euler angles are disqualifying for this mission profile**, in the report's words.

**Determinant −1.** Negating one axis at a time doesn't produce a rotation, it produces a *reflection*. A left-handed frame fed to a right-handed renderer mirrors everything. That's why fixing left/right broke front/back — I was searching a space that contained no solution.

## What changed

**Mahony filter on SO(3), replacing the Euler complementary filter.** Quaternion state, globally non-singular. The error term is the cross product of measured and predicted gravity — no trigonometry, no inverse, no singularity. `Kp = 1.0` keeps the same ~1 s cross-over you had; `Ki = 0.05` adds an explicit gyro-bias state that tracks thermal drift in flight, which Madgwick doesn't carry and an EKF would need Jacobians and matrix inversion to get.

**The quaternion is transmitted and rotates the model directly.** Euler never touches the display path. The rotation matrix from a unit quaternion always has determinant +1, so mirroring is now structurally impossible.

**AK09916 Y and Z negated.** The ICM-20948 is a multi-chip module — the magnetometer is a separate AKM die whose frame is rotated 180° about X from the inertial sensors. Note this is *not* the X/Y swap the MPU-9250 needed. Omitting it mirrors any tilt-compensated heading.

**Gyro DLPF 23.9 Hz → 11.6 Hz.** This was worse than a noise problem: at a 50 Hz poll, Nyquist is 25 Hz, and a 23.9 Hz corner gives essentially zero attenuation at fold-over. N20 gear whine and chute snatch above 25 Hz were aliasing into the baseband as fake rotation, unrecoverably.

**Sign constants deleted, replaced by a mounting DCM** (identity until you measure it) with the six-position procedure documented in `Config.h`. Measure it, check `det(M) == +1`, and the display is correct by construction.

## The budget problem I caught on myself

Adding the quaternion alongside the always-on gyro triple came to **112 B — over the wall**. I removed the gyro triple rather than ship that. It only existed to unfreeze the old display; the quaternion supersedes it. `SPIN_RATE` stays in the mandated core, `GX/GY/GZ` still rotate through the optional slot, and all three axes are on the SD card at 20 Hz, which §6.3.iii.C explicitly permits as the backup record.

## Two things not yet done

**Flash all three** — Teensy, ESP32, and restart the GCS. The wire format and CSV width both changed.

**The mounting matrix is still identity.** If the model moves correctly now, leave it. If an axis is still wrong, run the six-position test rather than guessing — that's the measurement that discriminates, and I shouldn't have skipped it three attempts ago.

Still outstanding from before: the ESP32 gateway remains uncompiled, and GNSS.

Look at post-restart — `Q3` monotonically drifting from `0.008` to `0.111` while stationary. That's yaw drift from a `-0.8°/s` gyro bias with no magnetometer reference to stop it. **That's why "once it starts moving nothing stops."**

# The 7Semi L89HA Multi-GNSS Breakout on a Teensy 4.1 — Complete Technical Guide
## TL;DR
- The 7Semi L89HA breakout carries a Quectel L89HA module built on an **STMicroelectronics Teseo III** GNSS engine (Quectel's L89 Hardware Design block diagram is captioned "Teseo ||| GNSS") — so it uses ST **$PSTM** NMEA-proprietary commands, **NOT** MediaTek PMTK commands. Any PMTK command list you find online will not work on this board.
- Wiring to a Teensy 4.1 is trivial: it is a pure 3.3V-logic NMEA-over-UART device (9600 baud 8N1 default). Cross TX↔RX to a hardware serial port (e.g. Serial1: pin 0 = RX1, pin 1 = TX1), share GND, power from 3.3V/VIN, and parse with TinyGPS++. No level shifting is needed.
- Out of the box it just works with `TinyGPSPlus` — the module streams GNGGA/GNRMC/GNGSA/GNGSV/GNVTG/GNGLL at 1 Hz and gets a fix with the built-in antennas or an external active antenna. Multi-constellation selection, 10 Hz, and PPS tuning require $PSTM commands.
## Key Findings
- **Chipset reality check.** The Quectel L89/L89HA is a dual-receiver GNSS module built around an ST Teseo III baseband. Per Quectel's L89 Hardware Design (Rev. V1.1), Table 2 "Supported Protocols" lists exactly two: "NMEA — ASCII, 0183, 3.01(default)/4.10" and "PSTM — ST proprietary protocols." This is the single most important correction to the user's brief, which assumed PMTK.
- **Two GNSS engines, dual band.** The module runs two receivers concurrently: an L1 patch-antenna path (GPS L1 / Galileo E1 / GLONASS L1 / BeiDou B1) and an L5 chip-antenna path (IRNSS/NavIC L5). Default factory configuration is GPS + Galileo + IRNSS.
- **Electrical.** Module VCC 3.1–4.3 V (3.3 V nominal), 150 mA peak. The 7Semi board adds a USB-C 5 V input and an onboard regulator; all logic pins are 3.3 V CMOS.
- **Confirmed broken-out pins** are VCC, GND, TX, RX, and BAT; SDA/SCL, PPS, RST and WAKE exist on the module but their exact silkscreen exposure on the breakout is not published by 7Semi.
- **Libraries.** TinyGPS++ (Mikal Hart) is the recommended parser on Teensy 4.1; 7Semi also ship their own thin `7semi_L89HA` NMEA-parsing library. The SparkFun u-blox library will NOT work (that is a UBX-protocol library for u-blox chips).
## Details
### 1. Module overview & specifications (Quectel L89 / L89HA)
The L89 is a high-performance IRNSS-enabled multi-GNSS module with two GNSS receivers, two LNAs, two SAW filters, a TCXO and a crystal oscillator, plus embedded flash for configuration/updates. The raw module measures 18.4 × 26.4 × 6.8 mm and weighs approximately 8.2 g, on 16 LCC pads (per the Quectel L89 spec sheet).
**Receiver / bands:**
- GPS L1 C/A 1575.42 MHz
- Galileo E1 C/A 1575.42 MHz
- GLONASS L1 C/A 1602.5625 MHz
- BeiDou B1 C/A 1561.098 MHz
- IRNSS/NavIC L5 C/A 1176.45 MHz
- QZSS L1; SBAS: WAAS/EGNOS/MSAS/GAGAN; also DGPS and AGPS
**Power supply:** VCC 3.1–4.3 V (typ 3.3 V), backup V_BCKP 1.9–4.3 V. Peak current 150 mA. Quectel recommends an LDO with ≥150 mA output.
**Current consumption (VCC = 3.3 V, −130 dBm):**
- GPS only: 91 mA acquisition / 78 mA tracking
- IRNSS only: 100 mA / 88 mA
- GPS+IRNSS: 102 mA / 100 mA
- GPS+Galileo+IRNSS: 99 mA / 95 mA
- Backup mode: 7 µA
**Sensitivity (GPS):** Acquisition −147 dBm, Reacquisition −156 dBm, Tracking −163 dBm.
**Sensitivity (IRNSS):** Acquisition −144 dBm, Reacquisition −154 dBm, Tracking −161 dBm. (The L89HA-S90 datasheet catalogs acquisition sensitivity as −147 dBm and cold-start TTFF as 32 s.)
**TTFF @ −130 dBm without AGPS:** Cold < 32 s, Warm < 25 s, Hot < 2 s. With AGPS: Cold < 13 s, Warm < 5 s, Hot < 2 s.
**Accuracy:** Horizontal position < 1.8 m CEP (autonomous). Velocity < 0.1 m/s, acceleration 0.1 m/s².
**Update rate:** 1 Hz default, max 10 Hz.
**1PPS:** Typical accuracy 3.9 ns, pulse width 500 ms, synchronized at rising edge.
**Dynamics:** max altitude 18,000 m, max velocity 515 m/s, acceleration 4 G.
**Temperature:** operation −40 °C to +85 °C (module); the 7Semi board is rated −40 °C to +70 °C.
**Antennas:** Embedded 18.4 × 18.4 × 4.0 mm patch antenna (L1, RHCP, ~4 dBi) and 10.0 × 3.2 × 4.0 mm chip antenna (IRNSS L5). Supports an external active antenna via EX_ANT (DC fed from VCC; antenna current must be 7–40 mA); internal/external paths are switched by an on-module SPDT. Antenna status is queried with $PSTMANTENNASTATUS.
**TCXO + RTC crystal** are on-module. **Backup battery** on V_BCKP keeps the RTC/SRAM alive for fast TTFF.
### 2. Complete pinout
**Raw Quectel L89 module (16 LCC pads):**
| Pin | Name | I/O | Function |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | RXD | DI | UART receive (into module) |
| 2 | TXD | DO | UART transmit (NMEA out) |
| 3 | GND | — | Ground |
| 4 | VCC | PI | Main supply 3.1–4.3 V |
| 5 | V_BCKP | PI | RTC backup supply 1.9–4.3 V |
| 6 | 1PPS | DO | Pulse per second, 500 ms wide, rising-edge sync; internal 47 kΩ pulldown |
| 7 | WAKE_UP | DI | Wake from backup; keep low in full-on; internal 51 kΩ pulldown |
| 8 | AADET_N | DO | Active-antenna detect (low = antenna present) |
| 9 | 3D_FIX/BOOT | IO | Fix indicator (high after fix) / boot-download select |
| 10 | RESET | DI | Active-low reset (≥10 ms); 1 V domain — no pull-up allowed |
| 11 | EX_ANT | AI | External active-antenna RF input, 50 Ω |
| 12 | GND | — | Ground |
| 13 | JAM_IND | DO | Jamming indicator (under development) |
| 14 | GEO_FENCE | DO | Geofence indicator (under development) |
| 15 | I2C_SCL | IO | I2C clock, internal 4.7 kΩ pullup to 3.0 V |
| 16 | I2C_SDA | IO | I2C data, internal 4.7 kΩ pullup to 3.0 V |
**7Semi breakout board:** 50 × 30 × 8 mm, USB-C 5 V input with onboard regulator, embedded patch + chip antennas. Confirmed header labels: **VCC, GND, TX, RX, BAT** (BAT = V_BCKP backup pin, per Robocraze's product description of the "L89HA Multi-GNSS Breakout Module — 7Semi"). The board also exposes I2C (SDA/SCL) per its "UART & I2C" spec. A U.FL/IPEX external-antenna connector and status-LED labeling are not documented by 7Semi. Default UART is 9600 8N1. The module's I2C 7-bit address is 0x3A (Quectel L89 Hardware Design V1.1: "Default I2C 7-bit address value: 0x3A… I2C_SCL and I2C_SDA have been pulled up to 3.0 V internally with 4.7 kΩ resistors").
### 3. Communication protocols
**UART:** 9600 baud default, 8 data bits, no parity, 1 stop bit, no flow control. Supported rates 4800–921600 bps. Used for NMEA output and firmware upgrade. CMOS 3.3 V levels only.
**I2C:** fast-mode up to 400 kbps, 7-bit slave address 0x3A, NMEA readable over I2C.
**Default NMEA output (NMEA 0183 v4.10 by default, v3.01 selectable):** RMC, VTG, GGA, GSA, GSV, GLL at 1 Hz. Talker IDs: GP (GPS), GL (GLONASS), GA (Galileo), GB/BD (BeiDou), GQ (QZSS), GI (IRNSS), GN (combined). On this module you typically see $GNRMC, $GNGGA, $GNGSA, $GNVTG, $GNGLL plus per-constellation $GxGSV.
**Command protocol is $PSTM (ST proprietary), not PMTK.** Structure: `$PSTM<command>[,args]*<checksum><CR><LF>`, where checksum is the XOR of all bytes between `$` and `*`. The `*<checksum>` is optional on input for most $PSTM commands.
Key operations:
- Cold/Warm/Hot start: `$PSTMCOLD` / `$PSTMWARM` / `$PSTMHOT`; system reset `$PSTMSRR`.
- Standby/backup: `$PSTMFORCESTANDBY,<duration_seconds>`; wake via WAKE_UP pin or timeout.
- Constellation config: `$PSTMCFGCONST,<gps>,<glonass>,<galileo>,<qzss>,<beidou>,<irnss>` where each field is 0 = off, 1 = tracked, 2 = tracked+used in positioning. Also `$PSTMSETCONSTMASK,<mask>` with a decimal bitmask — 7Semi's own product instructions state, verbatim: "To enable IRNSS please use the following AT command.: PSTMSETCONSTMASK,1024. Use External Active antenna with Open roof/sky."
- Update rate: `$PSTMSETPAR,1303,<seconds>` then `$PSTMSAVEPAR` then `$PSTMSRR`. 0.1 = 10 Hz; 0.2 = 5 Hz; 1 = 1 Hz.
- Save/restore: `$PSTMSAVEPAR` saves to NVM; `$PSTMRESTOREPAR` restores factory defaults; `$PSTMGETPAR` reads software version/parameters.
- Message list, ports, PPS, antenna: `$PSTMCFGMSGL`, `$PSTMCFGPORT`, `$PSTMPPS`, `$PSTMSETANTSENSOPMODE`, `$PSTMANTENNASTATUS`.
### 4. Wiring to Teensy 4.1
Teensy 4.1 is a 3.3 V board and its pins are **NOT 5 V tolerant** — but the L89 UART is already 3.3 V CMOS, so no level shifter is needed. Recommended connection using Serial1:
| L89HA breakout | Teensy 4.1 | Note |
|---|---|---|
| VCC | 3.3V (or VIN 5V if using the board's USB-C regulator) | 250 mA available on Teensy 3.3 V rail |
| GND | GND | common ground required |
| TX | Pin 0 (RX1) | module TX → Teensy RX |
| RX | Pin 1 (TX1) | Teensy TX → module RX |
| PPS (if exposed) | Pin 2 | any digital pin; all support attachInterrupt |
| BAT | 3 V coin cell + (optional) | keeps RTC alive for fast TTFF |
Teensy 4.1 hardware serial ports: Serial1 (RX 0 / TX 1), Serial2 (7/8), Serial3 (15/14), Serial4 (16/17), Serial5 (21/20), Serial6 (25/24), Serial7 (28/29), Serial8 (34/35). All have 4-byte FIFOs and interrupt buffering, ideal for GNSS at 10 Hz / 115200. If powering via VIN while also connecting USB, cut the VUSB–VIN trace on the underside to avoid back-feeding the host USB port.
Antenna placement: keep the patch antenna facing the sky, at least 20 mm from board edges and 10 mm from tall metal, with a clear ground plane. For indoor/urban use, an external active antenna on the roof/window gives far better fix reliability.
### 5. Libraries
- **TinyGPS++ (Mikal Hart)** — best choice on Teensy 4.1. Current versions parse GNRMC/GNGGA natively and give lat/lon/alt/speed/course/date/time/satellites/HDOP. Use with a hardware serial port. (Older TinyGPS forks needed a manual `_GNRMCterm`/`_GNGGAterm` patch to accept the "GN" talker ID — verify your version handles GN sentences.)
- **7semi_L89HA** — 7Semi's official thin library; parses GGA/RMC/GSV to give position, date/time, altitude and speed. MIT license, in the Arduino Library Manager, with a Basic example. Its README wiring table lists TX/RX/GND/VCC only.
- **Adafruit_GPS** — works as a raw NMEA reader; its PMTK helper methods are MediaTek-specific and will be silently ignored by the Teseo III, so use it only for NMEA reading.
- **NeoGPS** — very RAM-efficient, works well on Teensy; good for constrained/high-rate parsing.
- **SparkFun u-blox GNSS** — NOT compatible (UBX protocol, u-blox only).
### 6. Working code examples
**A. Raw NMEA passthrough (bring-up test), Teensy 4.1:**
```cpp
void setup() {
  Serial.begin(115200);
  Serial1.begin(9600);   // L89HA default
}
void loop() {
  if (Serial1.available()) Serial.write(Serial1.read());
  if (Serial.available())  Serial1.write(Serial.read()); // lets you type $PSTM cmds
}
```
**B. TinyGPS++ parse of lat/lon/alt/speed/time:**
```cpp
#include <TinyGPS++.h>
TinyGPSPlus gps;
#define GPS Serial1
void setup(){ Serial.begin(115200); GPS.begin(9600); }
void loop(){
  while (GPS.available()) gps.encode(GPS.read());
  if (gps.location.isUpdated()){
    Serial.printf("Lat %.6f Lon %.6f Alt %.1fm Spd %.2fkn Sats %d HDOP %.1f\n",
      gps.location.lat(), gps.location.lng(), gps.altitude.meters(),
      gps.speed.knots(), gps.satellites.value(), gps.hdop.hdop());
    Serial.printf("UTC %02d:%02d:%02d %02d/%02d/%04d\n",
      gps.time.hour(), gps.time.minute(), gps.time.second(),
      gps.date.day(), gps.date.month(), gps.date.year());
  }
}
```
**C. Sending $PSTM commands with computed checksum (10 Hz + save):**
```cpp
void sendPSTM(const char* body){        // body without $ or *
  uint8_t ck=0; for(const char*p=body;*p;p++) ck^=*p;
  Serial1.printf("$%s*%02X\r\n", body, ck);
}
void setup(){
  Serial.begin(115200); Serial1.begin(9600);
  delay(1500);
  sendPSTM("PSTMSETPAR,1303,0.1");  // 10 Hz fix rate
  sendPSTM("PSTMSAVEPAR");          // persist to NVM
  sendPSTM("PSTMSRR");              // reset to apply
}
void loop(){ if(Serial1.available()) Serial.write(Serial1.read()); }
```
**D. PPS interrupt + time sync:**
```cpp
volatile uint32_t ppsMicros=0; volatile bool pps=false;
void ppsISR(){ ppsMicros=micros(); pps=true; }
void setup(){
  Serial.begin(115200); Serial1.begin(9600);
  pinMode(2, INPUT);
  attachInterrupt(digitalPinToInterrupt(2), ppsISR, RISING);
}
void loop(){
  if(pps){ pps=false; Serial.printf("PPS edge at %lu us\n", ppsMicros); }
}
```
Combine with the NMEA time from GGA/RMC (or enable ZDA): the PPS rising edge marks the exact top of the UTC second, and the following NMEA sentence tells you which second it was. This is the exact pattern used by Teensy-4.1 NTP-server projects (ddrown/teensy-ntp, IMS-AS-LUH/t41-ptp).
**E. Selecting constellations (add GLONASS + BeiDou to the default GPS/Galileo/IRNSS):**
```cpp
// gps, glonass, galileo, qzss, beidou, irnss  (0=off,1=track,2=track+use)
sendPSTM("PSTMCFGCONST,2,2,2,1,2,2");
sendPSTM("PSTMSAVEPAR");
sendPSTM("PSTMSRR");
```
(Note the module's dual-receiver architecture and RF front-end constrain which constellations can run truly concurrently; verify with $PSTMGETPAR and the GSV/GSA output.)
### 7. $PSTM command reference (selected)
| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| $PSTMCOLD / $PSTMWARM / $PSTMHOT | Cold / warm / hot start |
| $PSTMSRR | System reset |
| $PSTMSETCONSTMASK,<mask> | Set constellation bitmask (decimal; 1024 = IRNSS per 7Semi) |
| $PSTMCFGCONST,<gps>,<glo>,<gal>,<qzss>,<bds>,<irnss> | Per-constellation 0/1/2 config |
| $PSTMSETPAR,1303,<sec> + $PSTMSAVEPAR + $PSTMSRR | Set fix/update rate (0.1 = 10 Hz) |
| $PSTMCFGMSGL | Configure NMEA message list |
| $PSTMCFGPORT | Configure UART port / baud |
| $PSTMFORCESTANDBY,<sec> | Enter backup/standby |
| $PSTMPPS,… | Configure 1PPS behaviour |
| $PSTMSAVEPAR / $PSTMRESTOREPAR / $PSTMGETPAR | Save / factory-restore / read params |
| $PSTMANTENNASTATUS | Query external antenna open/normal/short |
| $PSTMINITGPS / $PSTMINITTIME | Seed position/time for faster TTFF |
Checksum = XOR of all characters between `$` and `*`, printed as two hex digits.
### 8. Troubleshooting & best practices
- **No fix indoors:** GNSS needs sky view. First cold fix can take up to ~32 s (longer with weak signal). Use the external active antenna near a window/roof for indoor benches.
- **No NMEA at all / garbage:** confirm 9600 8N1, TX↔RX crossed, common ground, and 3.3 V logic (never feed 5 V into Teensy pins).
- **$PSTM commands ignored:** they need correct `\r\n` line endings and (for some) a valid checksum; changes to rate/constellation require `$PSTMSAVEPAR` + `$PSTMSRR` to take effect and persist.
- **Slow TTFF every boot:** keep BAT/V_BCKP powered (coin cell) so the RTC/almanac survive; consider AGPS or `$PSTMINITGPS` seeding.
- **10 Hz but stale data:** the Teseo solution rate is limited; at 10 Hz you may see repeated/interpolated fixes when signal is weak. Confirm timestamps increase by 0.1 s each fix.
- **Power noise:** give VCC a clean regulated 3.3 V with 10 µF + 100 nF decoupling; keep DC-DC converters away from the antenna to preserve sensitivity.
- **Serial overflow at high rates:** Teensy 4.1 hardware UART FIFOs handle this well; call `gps.encode()` continuously in loop and avoid long blocking delays.
### 9. 7Semi's own documentation
7Semi's product page links the Quectel L89 datasheet, an L89 AT-command manual, a CM-10119 3D STEP file, and Windows tools (QCOM, QFlash, QNavigator). Their official Arduino library is `7semi-solutions/7Semi-L89HA-GNSS-Module-Arduino-Library` on GitHub (MIT, v1.0.1, released Aug 2025), with a Basic example and a 4-pin wiring table (TX/RX/GND/VCC). There is no 7Semi-published full breakout schematic or silkscreen pin map beyond that. Board SKU is CM-10119; list price ≈ US$14.12 / ₹999–1,178.
### 10. Real-world examples
- **Robocraze** published a full L89HA + Arduino tutorial using SoftwareSerial + TinyGPS++ (the same approach ported here to Teensy hardware serial), and sells the board as the "L89HA Multi-GNSS Breakout Module — 7Semi," describing concurrent L1/L5 reception and the BAT backup pin.
- **Quectel and ST community forums** document $PSTM usage, IRNSS-only via `$PSTMSETCONSTMASK,1024`, and 10 Hz via `$PSTMSETPAR,1303,0.1` (with the caveat that the observed rate can differ before a valid fix).
- **PJRC Teensy forums / GitHub** have multiple GPS-PPS NTP-server projects on Teensy 4.1 (ddrown/teensy-ntp, IMS-AS-LUH/t41-ptp) showing PPS-interrupt + ZDA/RMC time-discipline patterns directly reusable here.
## Recommendations
1. **Bring-up (day 1):** wire VCC→3.3 V, GND→GND, TX→pin 0, RX→pin 1; upload sketch A at 9600; confirm NMEA. If nothing appears, swap TX/RX and re-check ground.
2. **Get a fix:** run sketch B with TinyGPS++, take the board outside or use an external active antenna; wait up to ~1–2 minutes for the first cold fix.
3. **Tune (day 2):** use sketch C to set 10 Hz and `$PSTMSAVEPAR`; if you need specific constellations (add BeiDou/GLONASS, or IRNSS-only), use sketch E / `$PSTMSETCONSTMASK`. Verify with `$PSTMGETPAR`.
4. **Timing projects:** add PPS to an interrupt pin (sketch D) and discipline millis()/micros() to PPS + NMEA time; enable ZDA if you want a clean date/time sentence.
5. **Thresholds that change the plan:** if TTFF or indoor reliability is unacceptable, escalate to an external active antenna first, then AGPS / `$PSTMINITGPS` seeding, before considering a different module. If you truly need concurrent 4+ full constellations in positioning, verify the Teseo III's front-end supports your combination via GSA/GSV before committing.
## Caveats
- This module is **Teseo III / $PSTM**, not MediaTek/PMTK. Discard any PMTK command guide — those commands will be ignored.
- The exact silkscreen pin list of the 7Semi breakout beyond VCC/GND/TX/RX/BAT, the presence of a U.FL connector, the onboard LDO part, any level shifting, and status-LED labels are **not documented publicly**; confirm against the physical board. The onboard 5 V→3.3 V regulation is inferred from the USB-C 5 V input plus 3.3 V module requirement, not explicitly stated by 7Semi.
- The Cirkit Designer "L89HA Breakout" page contains a generic/incorrect ATmega-style pin table (digital I/O, PWM, 6 analog pins, 16 MHz, 32 KB flash) that does **not** match a GNSS module — treat it as unreliable.
- `$PSTMSETPAR` CDB IDs (e.g. 1303 for fix rate) and exact fix-rate behavior are drawn from ST Teseo community threads and the L89 protocol spec; validate on your specific firmware version with `$PSTMGETPAR`.
- Marketing copy across resellers occasionally conflates module-level specs (3.3 V, I2C address, embedded antennas) with board-level behavior; where this guide states "confirmed," it reflects the Quectel datasheets, 7Semi's own listing/library, or ST/Quectel forums.

"# Attitude Estimation with the 7Semi ICM-20948 on a Teensy 4.1 CanSat Flight Computer: A Complete Engineering Guide
## TL;DR
- The 7Semi ICM-20948 breakout (SKU BO-10167, 21×17×2 mm; Evelta ₹1,132.80 incl. GST / ₹960 ex-GST, IndiaMart/Robocraze ~₹906, ~$11.04 at 7semi.com where it is currently Sold Out) is a bare 3.3 V-only board carrying the TDK InvenSense ICM-20948 — a two-die multi-chip module pairing an accel/gyro/DMP die with the Asahi Kasei AK09916 magnetometer whose Y and Z axes are inverted relative to the accel/gyro. For a CanSat you MUST apply `my=-my; mz=-mz` (the "Multi-Chip Module trap") and derive a mounting matrix, or your ground-station cube will mirror.
- For real flight — 30 g ejection, sustained spin, vertical descent — DISABLE the DMP (its 9-axis/geomagnetic modes are buggy, NDA-encumbered, and mis-align yaw), run raw registers at ≥225 Hz on the Teensy, and use a Mahony filter with dynamic gain gating for shock rejection. Add external 2.2 kΩ pull-ups to 3.3 V on the Teensy 4.1 I²C lines because PJRC states the internal pull-ups on Teensy 4.1 are "very weak" and unreliable at 400 kHz.
- Transmit unit quaternions (w,x,y,z as 4 floats) at 10–50 Hz plus a calibration-status byte; reconstruct orientation on the ground with a right-handed frame and `q_display = inverse(q_sensor)` to avoid the left-handed mirroring pitfall. Calibrate gyro bias (static average), accelerometer (6-position), and magnetometer (ellipsoid fit) before every flight.
## Key Findings
1. **The 7Semi board is a minimal 3.3 V breakout, not a SparkFun-style level-shifted board.** 7semi.com lists "VOLTAGE REQUIRE: 3.3V", dimensions "21 X 17 X 2" mm, interface "SPI/I2C", and describes it as operating "within a 1.95V to 3.6V supply range." No onboard regulator, no level shifter, and no Qwiic connector are documented — unlike the SparkFun board, which explicitly "incorporates... a logic shifter." Treat the 7Semi board as 3.3 V-only. As Wolles Elektronikkiste warns for the bare chip: "the ICM-20948 can only be operated with up to 3.6 volts. However, this only applies to VDD. The I/O pins and VDDIO can only tolerate 1.71 – 1.95 volts." The Teensy 4.1 is natively 3.3 V, a good match.
2. **The default I²C address is 0x68/0x69 (7-bit), NOT 0x29.** The 7Semi GitHub README erroneously says "Default I²C address: 0x29" (a copy-paste from another sensor library); the working example uses `beginI2C(0x69,...)`. AD0 low → 0x68, AD0 high → 0x69. WHO_AM_I (register 0x00, Bank 0) returns 0xEA.
3. **The magnetometer die is rotated relative to the inertial die.** The accel/gyro follow a right-handed frame (flat: 0 g X, 0 g Y, +1 g Z). The AK09916 X aligns with accel X, but its Y and Z are reversed. The widely used jremington/ICM_20948-AHRS reference applies, verbatim, `Mxyz[1] = -Mxyz[1]; //reflect Y and Z` and `Mxyz[2] = -Mxyz[2]; //must be done after offsets & scales applied to raw data`.
4. **The DMP is a trap for CanSat.** SparkFun's DMP.md states they received a "confidential & proprietary document called 'Application Note: Programming Sequence for ICM-20648 DMP Hardware Function'" that InvenSense "asked us not to share it openly," yielding only "partial support." The firmware image is 14,301 bytes (`#define DMP_CODE_SIZE 14301` in ICM_20948_C.h). Multiple GitHub issues document 9-axis quaternion yaw that ignores the magnetic field, ~10 s settling in geomagnetic mode, and yaw 90° misaligned with roll/pitch. Use raw registers + your own Mahony filter instead.
5. **Teensy 4.1 needs external I²C pull-ups.** PJRC's Wire page states: "On Teensy 2.0, 4.0, and 4.1, the weak internal pullup resistors may be sufficient for short wires to a single device… The on-chip pullup resistors in Teensy 2.0, Teensy 4.0, Teensy 4.1 are very weak." Sensirion's bench test confirms that "with the strong pull-up with 2.2 kOhm resistor the signal looks quite rectangular," whereas at "400 kHz… if the internal pull-ups are turned on. No signal was detected anymore at all." Add 2.2 kΩ to 3.3 V on SDA (pin 18) and SCL (pin 19), or use SPI (up to 7 MHz) for the flight build.
## Details
### 1. The 7Semi ICM-20948 Breakout — Hardware Overview
- **Identity & price:** 7Semi SKU **BO-10167** ("ICM-20948 9DoF IMU Breakout (MPU-9250 Upgrade)"), made by Idisonic Creations Pvt Ltd, Navi Mumbai. Manufacturer price $11.04 (currently Sold Out at 7semi.com). Indian resellers: Evelta ₹1,132.80 incl. GST (₹960 ex-GST, "16 in stock," "Shipped in 24 Hours from Mumbai Warehouse"), Robocraze ~₹1,130 (IndiaMart listing ₹906.00, ID 2856420231312), Vishaworld ₹1,132 incl. GST. Robu.in does NOT stock the 7Semi board (only the SparkFun SEN-15335 at ~₹2,965).
- **Dimensions:** 21 × 17 × 2 mm; weight ~4 g. Mechanical DXF/STEP/GLB files are published at 7semi.com/content/3d-files/10236.{dxf,STEP,glb}. Mounting-hole presence is not documented in text; inspect the DXF/STEP.
- **Power:** Documented as 3.3 V; description quotes the chip's 1.95–3.6 V VDD range. No regulator or level-shifting is documented — do NOT connect 5 V logic.
- **Interfaces:** I²C (100/400 kHz) and SPI (up to 7 MHz). Broken out on 0.1"-pitch headers "clearly labeled I2C/SPI." The library references VCC, GND, SDA, SCL, AD0 (I²C); SCK, MOSI, MISO, CS (SPI); INT (interrupt/wake-on-motion); and AUX_DA/AUX_CL (auxiliary bus / bypass). FSYNC is a chip pin (tie to GND if unused). Exact silkscreen labels require reading the product photos/DXF.
- **Onboard pull-ups:** value not documented. Assume you must supply external pull-ups on the Teensy bus.
- **Library & repo:** `github.com/7semi-solutions/7Semi_ICM20948_Arduino-Library` (MIT, v1.0.0, Dec 2025). Also on PlatformIO/Arduino Library Manager as "7Semi ICM20948." It supports I²C + SPI, DLPF, FSR, ODR, and magnetometer via internal I²C master (`readMag()` after `initMag()`), but it does NOT implement DMP or sensor fusion — you add the filter.
**7Semi board wiring to Teensy 4.1 (I²C, recommended for bring-up):**
| ICM-20948 pin | Teensy 4.1 pin | Note |
|---|---|---|
| VCC | 3.3V | 3.3 V only |
| GND | GND | common ground |
| SDA | 18 (SDA0) | add 2.2 kΩ → 3.3 V |
| SCL | 19 (SCL0) | add 2.2 kΩ → 3.3 V |
| AD0 | GND or 3.3V | 0x68 (low) / 0x69 (high) |
| INT | any GPIO (e.g., 2) | data-ready interrupt |
| FSYNC | GND | unused |
**SPI (recommended for flight, up to 7 MHz):** VCC→3.3V, GND→GND, SCK→13, MOSI→11, MISO→12, CS→10 (any GPIO). SPI avoids the pull-up problem entirely and gives deterministic timing.
### 2. ICM-20948 Chip — TDK InvenSense Specs (DS-000189, rev 1.6, 03/12/2024)
- **Package:** 24-pin QFN, 3×3×1 mm, 20,000 g shock tolerant. Two dies: (a) 3-axis gyro + 3-axis accel + DMP; (b) AK09916 magnetometer. Exposed die pad must NOT be soldered.
- **Gyroscope:** FSR ±250/±500/±1000/±2000 dps; sensitivity 131 / 65.5 / 32.8 / 16.4 LSB/dps; 16-bit; noise spectral density 0.015 dps/√Hz; ZRO tolerance ±5 dps; ZRO drift ±0.05 dps/°C; ODR up to 1.125 kHz (low-noise) or 9 kHz (FCHOICE=0).
- **Accelerometer:** FSR ±2/±4/±8/±16 g; sensitivity 16,384 / 8,192 / 4,096 / 2,048 LSB/g; 16-bit two's complement; noise 230 µg/√Hz; zero-g board-level tolerance ±50 mg; ODR up to 1.125 kHz.
- **Magnetometer (AK09916):** FSR ±4900 µT; 16-bit; sensitivity 0.15 µT/LSB; ODR 100 Hz continuous; initial zero-field tolerance ±2000 LSB; compass temp range −30 to +85 °C.
- **Temperature sensor:** 333.87 LSB/°C; T(°C) = (raw − RoomOffset)/333.87 + 21.
- **Supply:** VDD 1.71–3.6 V; VDDIO 1.71–1.95 V (different from MPU-9250!). 9-axis current ~3.11 mA (DMP off); sleep 8 µA.
- **Chip pinout (QFN):** 7 AUX_CL, 8 VDDIO, 9 AD0/SDO, 10 REGOUT (needs 0.1 µF cap), 11 FSYNC, 12 INT1, 13 VDD, 18 GND, 21 AUX_DA, 22 nCS, 23 SCL/SCLK, 24 SDA/SDI.
**Register map (banked — REG_BANK_SEL = 0x7F selects bank via bits [5:4]: 0x00/0x10/0x20/0x30):**
- **Bank 0:** WHO_AM_I 0x00 (=0xEA), USER_CTRL 0x03 (bit7 DMP_EN, bit5 I2C_MST_EN, bit0 reset), LP_CONFIG 0x05, PWR_MGMT_1 0x06 (bit7 DEVICE_RESET, bit6 SLEEP, bits[2:0] CLKSEL), PWR_MGMT_2 0x07 (accel/gyro standby bits), INT_PIN_CFG 0x0F (bit1 BYPASS_EN), INT_ENABLE_1 0x11 (RAW_DATA_RDY), ACCEL_XOUT_H 0x2D … GYRO_ZOUT_L, TEMP_OUT_H/L, EXT_SLV_SENS_DATA_00 0x3B (magnetometer lands here).
- **Bank 1:** self-test, XA/YA/ZA_OFFS accel offsets.
- **Bank 2:** GYRO_SMPLRT_DIV 0x00, GYRO_CONFIG_1 0x01 (bits[2:1] GYRO_FS_SEL, bits[5:3] DLPFCFG, bit0 FCHOICE), GYRO_CONFIG_2, XG/YG/ZG_OFFS_USR gyro offsets, ODR_ALIGN_EN 0x09, ACCEL_SMPLRT_DIV_1/2 0x10/0x11, ACCEL_CONFIG 0x14 (bits[2:1] ACCEL_FS_SEL, bits[5:3] DLPFCFG, bit0 FCHOICE), TEMP_CONFIG 0x53.
- **Bank 3:** I2C_MST_ODR_CONFIG 0x00, I2C_MST_CTRL 0x01 (400 kHz = 0x07), I2C_MST_DELAY_CTRL 0x02, I2C_SLV0_ADDR 0x03, I2C_SLV0_REG 0x04, I2C_SLV0_CTRL 0x05, I2C_SLV0_DO 0x06.
- **AK09916 registers (I²C addr 0x0C):** WIA2 0x01 (=0x09), ST1 0x10 (bit0 DRDY), HXL 0x11 … HZH 0x16 (little-endian, unlike main sensors), ST2 0x18 (must be read to latch; bit3 HOFL overflow), CNTL2 0x31 (0x08 = 100 Hz continuous mode 4), CNTL3 0x32 (bit0 SRST).
### 3. AXIS CONVENTIONS — The Critical Section
**Inertial die (accel + gyro):** right-handed frame per DS-000189 §15 (Figure 12). Flat on a table it reads 0 g X, 0 g Y, **+1 g Z** — the datasheet states "When the device is placed on a flat surface, it will measure 0g on the X- and Y-axes and +1g on the Z-axis." Gyro polarity follows the right-hand rule about each axis (positive rotation about +Z is counter-clockwise viewed from +Z looking down toward the board).
**Magnetometer die (AK09916):** rotated 180° about X relative to the inertial die. Datasheet Figure 13 ("Orientation of Axes of Sensitivity for Magnetometer") differs from Figure 12. In practice: **mag_X ∥ accel_X, mag_Y = −accel_Y, mag_Z = −accel_Z.** This is the "Multi-Chip Module trap." The universally applied fix (after subtracting hard-iron offset and applying soft-iron scale) is:
```
mx =  mx_raw;
my = -my_raw;   // reflect Y
mz = -mz_raw;   // reflect Z
```
If you skip this, tilt-compensated heading will be wrong and the sign of yaw will flip.
**Aerospace body frames:**
- **NED (North-East-Down):** +X nose/forward, +Y right wing, +Z down. Standard in flight dynamics; yaw increases clockwise from North.
- **ENU (East-North-Up):** common in robotics/ROS; +Z up.
- **NWU (North-West-Up):** used by the Mahony reference implementation (per the jremington README: "Standard sensor orientation… is X North (yaw=0) Y West and Z Up (NWU system)").
**Choose ONE and commit.** For a CanSat that descends vertically and spins about its long axis, mount the board so the **cylinder long axis = body Z**, nose = body X. Decide NED vs ENU and keep it identical in flight code and the ground station.
**Mounting matrix R_mount (6-position derivation):** The IMU is rarely aligned perfectly with the CanSat body. Define `a_body = R_mount · a_sensor`. Derive R_mount by placing the assembled CanSat in six precise orientations (±X, ±Y, ±Z pointing down). In each, the measured gravity vector identifies which sensor axis maps to which body axis and its sign; the collected columns form R_mount (a signed permutation matrix for a clean 90° mount, or a full DCM if skewed). Apply R_mount to accel, gyro, AND the axis-corrected magnetometer before fusion.
### 4. Movement Interpretation — Detailed Math
**What MEMS accelerometers measure:** *specific force* (proper acceleration), i.e., the reaction to gravity plus kinematic acceleration — NOT kinematic acceleration alone. At rest the proof mass is held up against gravity, so a level board reads **+1 g on the up-axis** (the sensor "feels" the normal force). In free-fall (the descent transient before the parachute loads) all axes read ~0 g.
**From a level start (a ≈ [0,0,+1] g):**
| Motion / tilt | Accel response |
|---|---|
| Tilt nose down (pitch −) | +X gains −g·sinθ; Z drops |
| Tilt right (roll +) | +Y gains +g·sinφ; Z drops |
| Accelerate forward (+X) | −X transient (reaction), returns to baseline |
| Free-fall | all axes → 0 g |
| 30 g ejection along −Z | Z saturates at −16 g rail if FSR too low |
At ±16 g FSR (2,048 LSB/g), +1 g ≈ +2,048 LSB. A 30 g event saturates ±16 g — see Troubleshooting.
**Tilt from accelerometer (units cancel, no scaling needed):**
```
roll  φ = atan2(ay, az)
pitch θ = atan2(-ax, sqrt(ay*ay + az*az))
```
Valid only quasi-statically (accel ≈ gravity). During spin/thrust these are corrupted — that is exactly why you fuse with the gyro.
**Tilt-compensated heading (after axis correction & calibration):**
```
mx2 = mx*cos(θ) + mz*sin(θ)
my2 = mx*sin(φ)*sin(θ) + my*cos(φ) - mz*sin(φ)*cos(θ)
yaw ψ = atan2(-my2, mx2)          // add magnetic declination for true North
```
**Gyro integration (attitude propagation):** angular position is the time integral of rate; do this in quaternion form, not Euler, to avoid singularities:
```
q̇ = ½ q ⊗ (0, ωx, ωy, ωz)
q(t+dt) = normalize(q + q̇·dt)
```
**Quaternion basics (w,x,y,z = q0,q1,q2,q3), unit norm q0²+q1²+q2²+q3²=1.**
**Rotation matrix (DCM) from quaternion (body→world):**
```
R = | 1-2(y²+z²)   2(xy-wz)     2(xz+wy)   |
    | 2(xy+wz)     1-2(x²+z²)   2(yz-wx)   |
    | 2(xz-wy)     2(yz+wx)     1-2(x²+y²) |
```
**Euler (Z-Y-X aerospace 3-2-1) from quaternion:**
```
roll  φ = atan2(2(wx+yz), 1-2(x²+y²))
pitch θ = asin(clamp(2(wy-zx), -1, 1))
yaw   ψ = atan2(2(wz+xy), 1-2(y²+z²))
```
The `asin` gives gimbal lock at θ=±90°; keep the quaternion as the primary state and derive Euler only for display.
### 5. Register-Level Setup (raw, no library) — I²C
1. Select Bank 0 (write 0x00 to 0x7F).
2. PWR_MGMT_1 (0x06) = 0x80 to reset; wait 100 ms; re-select Bank 0.
3. Read WHO_AM_I (0x00) — expect 0xEA.
4. PWR_MGMT_1 = 0x01 (wake, auto-select PLL clock).
5. PWR_MGMT_2 (0x07) = 0x00 (enable all accel + gyro axes).
6. Bank 2: GYRO_CONFIG_1 (0x01) set FS_SEL + DLPFCFG + FCHOICE=1 (e.g., 0x19 = 2000 dps, DLPF on); ACCEL_CONFIG (0x14) set FS_SEL=±16 g + DLPF; GYRO_SMPLRT_DIV (0x00) and ACCEL_SMPLRT_DIV_1/2 for the target ODR (e.g., div=4 → 225 Hz).
7. Magnetometer via internal I²C master: Bank 0 USER_CTRL (0x03) bit5 I2C_MST_EN=1; Bank 3 I2C_MST_CTRL (0x01)=0x07 (400 kHz); configure I2C_SLV0_ADDR=0x0C|0x80 (read), I2C_SLV0_REG=0x11 (HXL), I2C_SLV0_CTRL=0x80|len (enable + bytes). First write CNTL2 (0x31)=0x08 on the AK09916 (100 Hz continuous) via SLV0 in write mode. Mag bytes then auto-populate EXT_SLV_SENS_DATA_00+.
8. INT_PIN_CFG (0x0F) and INT_ENABLE_1 (0x11)=0x01 for a data-ready interrupt on INT1.
DLPF choice matters for **Nyquist**: set the bandwidth below half the ODR. For 225 Hz ODR use DLPF ≈ 50–100 Hz. Do not run wide-open bandwidth at low ODR or vibration aliases into the band.
### 6. Communication Pipeline
- **I²C:** 400 kHz recommended; external 2.2 kΩ pull-ups to 3.3 V on Teensy pins 18/19 mandatory for reliability. Burst-read 6 accel + 6 gyro + 2 temp bytes from 0x2D, plus 8 mag bytes (HXL..ST2) from EXT_SLV_SENS_DATA.
- **Endianness:** accel/gyro/temp are **big-endian** (H then L). The AK09916 mag is **little-endian** (L then H) — a classic bug source.
- **int16 → physical:** `value_g = raw_accel / 2048.0` (±16 g); `value_dps = raw_gyro / 16.4` (±2000 dps); `value_uT = raw_mag * 0.15`.
- **Rates:** gyro/accel up to 1.125 kHz; mag fixed 100 Hz — the mag is your fusion bottleneck.
- **SPI alternative:** up to 7 MHz, mode 0/3, MSB-first; set I2C_IF_DIS to lock to SPI. Preferred for the flight build (deterministic, no pull-up dependency).
- **Interrupt-driven vs polled:** use the INT1 data-ready line to trigger reads at a fixed cadence; avoids jitter in `dt` that corrupts gyro integration.
### 7. Sensor Fusion for CanSat Attitude
**Complementary filter (cheap baseline):** `angle = α(angle + gyro·dt) + (1−α)·accel_angle`, α≈0.98. Good for pitch/roll but no drift-free yaw without the mag.
**Mahony filter (RECOMMENDED for CanSat):** a quaternion complementary filter on SO(3). It computes the error between measured gravity/mag directions and those predicted by the current quaternion (via cross products), feeds that error through a PI controller (Kp, Ki) to correct the gyro, then integrates. Advantages for CanSat: cheap (no sqrt-heavy Jacobian like Madgwick's original), tunable gain for shock gating, explicit integral term that tracks gyro bias. Per the jremington README, its implementation "uses as reference directions Up and West (acceleration vector cross magnetic field vector)… It appears to converge more rapidly than the conventional Mahony approach (as coded by SOH Madgwick)."
Pseudocode (9-axis):
```
// normalize a and m; compute
halfe = (a × v_hat) + (m × w_hat)   // v_hat, w_hat = predicted directions
bias += Ki * halfe * dt
g_corrected = gyro + Kp * halfe + bias
q += 0.5 * q ⊗ (0, g_corrected) * dt
q = normalize(q)
```
**Madgwick filter:** gradient-descent correction; slightly better transient at higher compute cost; single β gain. Fine on the Teensy 4.1 (600 MHz M7) but harder to gate for shock.
**EKF:** best accuracy, tracks bias as state; heavy and error-prone to tune for a student CanSat. Overkill unless you need cm/deg-grade nav.
**Why quaternions:** no gimbal lock, cheap composition, direct to render. Keep q as state; convert to Euler only for human telemetry.
**Gimbal lock at ±90° pitch:** unavoidable in Euler; a vertically descending CanSat sits near pitch ±90° much of the flight, so quaternion state is essential. Detect |pitch|>85° and flag Euler as unreliable in telemetry.
**Shock rejection (30 g ejection) — dynamic gain gating:** when |‖a‖ − 1 g| exceeds a threshold (e.g., >0.3 g) or the gyro exceeds a rate limit, temporarily set the accel/mag correction gain to 0 (gyro-only propagation). This prevents the ejection transient and spin-induced centripetal acceleration from corrupting the attitude. Restore gain when ‖a‖ settles.
**Bias tracking:** the Mahony Ki integral continuously estimates gyro bias in flight; freeze it during high-dynamics windows.
### 8. Calibration Procedures
- **Gyro bias:** average ~2000 samples (≈10–60 s) held perfectly still; subtract the mean rate. Redo before each flight (bias drifts with temperature at ±0.05 dps/°C).
- **Accel 6-position:** place each axis ±up; record; solve for per-axis offset and scale so each 1 g face reads ±1.000 g. Store offsets in Bank 1 XA/YA/ZA_OFFS or apply in software.
- **Magnetometer hard/soft iron (ellipsoid fit):** rotate slowly through all 3D orientations collecting ~300+ points; fit an ellipsoid; the center = hard-iron offset (subtract), the inverse-square-root of the shape matrix = soft-iron 3×3 correction. Tools: `magneto`, jremington's `calibrate3.py`, or MATLAB `ellipsoid_fit`. Apply correction, THEN the Y/Z axis reflection. Do this with the fully assembled CanSat powered on (batteries/motors create hard-iron distortion) — "in-situ" calibration is mandatory.
- **Temperature compensation:** log die temp with each cal. For a short CanSat flight a fresh pre-launch gyro cal usually suffices.
- **Allan variance:** collect several hours static and compute the Allan deviation to characterize angle random walk and bias instability — useful for tuning Kp/Ki but optional for a first flight.
### 9. Telemetry for the Ground Station
- **Payload:** quaternion (4×float32 = 16 B) as primary; optionally Euler (3×float32) for humans; raw a/g/m (9×int16) for post-flight; a calibration-status byte; a millis timestamp.
- **Binary vs ASCII:** use a packed binary packet (little-endian float32) with a sync word and CRC for the RF link — far more bandwidth-efficient and robust than CSV/ASCII. ASCII only for USB bench debug.
- **Rate:** 10–50 Hz telemetry is plenty for visualization even if the filter runs at 225 Hz internally.
- **Time sync:** stamp each packet with the flight-computer millis; optionally discipline to GPS PPS if present.
- **Rendering:** feed the quaternion to a 3D cube in Unity/OpenGL/VPython/three.js.
- **Left-handed frame pitfall:** Unity uses a LEFT-handed coordinate system (Y up, Z forward); most IMU math is RIGHT-handed. If the cube mirrors or spins backwards, you have a handedness mismatch. Fixes: negate one axis and the corresponding quaternion components, apply `transform.rotation = Quaternion.Inverse(q_sensor)` (the filter outputs earth-relative-to-sensor, you want sensor-relative-to-earth), or swap axes explicitly (e.g., Unity (x,y,z,w) = sensor (x, z, y, −w)). Validate with a physical test: tilt nose down and confirm the cube's nose dips.
### 10–12. Working Code, Libraries, and Repositories
**Recommended library stack for Teensy 4.1:**
- **SparkFun_ICM-20948_ArduinoLibrary** — most complete, portable C backbone, magnetometer through internal master, partial DMP. Best documented. Works on Teensy 4.1 with the standard `Wire`. Use `ICM_20948_I2C` at 400 kHz. Use its raw AGMT output and add your own Mahony (do NOT rely on its DMP).
- **Adafruit_ICM20948 / ICM20X** — simplest Unified-Sensor API; good for bring-up; less register control.
- **jremington `ICM_20948-AHRS`** — the reference Mahony + tilt-compass sketch built ON TOP of the SparkFun library; includes the critical `Mxyz[1]=-Mxyz[1]; Mxyz[2]=-Mxyz[2];` axis reconciliation, gyro/accel/mag calibration sketches, and Python ellipsoid fit. Best starting point for a CanSat.
- **drcpattison/ICM-20948 (DPEng)** — a port of Kris Winer's MPU-9250 library with full Mahony AND Madgwick implementations and register-level init (PWR_MGMT_1, REG_BANK_SEL, GYRO_CONFIG_1=0x19, etc.).
- **7Semi_ICM20948_Arduino-Library** — the vendor's own; clean `readAccel/readGyro/readMag`, DLPF/FSR/ODR setters, but NO fusion and a documentation bug on the address. Fine for data-only.
- **Bolder Flight Systems / WickedLukas / mokhwasomssi (STM32 HAL)** — alternatives.
**Community references:** jremington ICM_20948-AHRS (Mahony); drcpattison/ICM-20948 and jonacappelle Master-Thesis (Madgwick source); savazeb/madgwick; Rohan More Medium walkthrough; SparkFun hookup guide + DMP.md; Adafruit ICM-20948 learn guide; Wolles Elektronikkiste ICM20948_WE (two-part deep dive); PJRC Teensy Wire/I²C threads. GitHub topic searches "icm-20948 madgwick," "icm-20948 teensy," "cansat sensor-fusion" surface additional flight-computer repos.
### 13. DMP (Digital Motion Processor)
The DMP runs InvenSense motion algorithms on-chip (~200 Hz internal, output to FIFO up to 225 Hz), including 6-axis and 9-axis quaternions. BUT: the 14,301-byte firmware image and its programming sequence are behind an InvenSense NDA; SparkFun reverse-engineered only PARTIAL support (raw/calibrated a/g/m, 6-axis and 9-axis Game Rotation Vector). Documented bugs: 9-axis quaternion yaw that does NOT respond to magnetic field (issues #56, #81); ORIENTATION/ROTATION_VECTOR modes that keep roll/pitch fixed at 0° and only GEOMAGNETIC mode gives absolute tilt but takes >10 s to settle (issue #59); yaw 90° misaligned with roll/pitch (issue #145); power-up drift over several seconds (issue #40). **Conclusion (matches the user's document): disable the DMP; run raw registers + your own Mahony filter.** You get deterministic timing, shock gating, and full control.
### 14. Troubleshooting
- **I²C lockup at 400 kHz on Teensy 4.1:** internal pull-ups are "very weak" (PJRC). Add external 2.2 kΩ to 3.3 V, keep wires short, or move to SPI.
- **Magnetometer silent / stuck values:** you didn't enable the internal I²C master (USER_CTRL bit5) or left BYPASS on; or you didn't read ST2 to latch a new sample; or you forgot the L-before-H little-endian order; or the mag is capped by HOFL overflow near a magnet.
- **Mirrored/inverted axes:** you skipped the mag Y/Z reflection or R_mount; cube mirrors on the ground → handedness mismatch (Section 9).
- **DLPF / Nyquist aliasing:** bandwidth set above ODR/2 lets spin vibration alias into attitude — set DLPF below half the sample rate.
- **Shock saturation at 30 g:** ±16 g FSR rails during ejection. Even at ±16 g a 30 g pulse clips; gate the filter (Section 7) during the event and rely on the gyro, then recover. Don't try to integrate clipped accel.
- **WHO_AM_I returns 0xE0 not 0xEA:** you're reading an ICM-20648 or a bad part / wrong bank; re-select Bank 0.
### 15. 7Semi-Specific Notes
- Product page (7semi.com/icm-20948-9dof-imu-breakout-mpu-9250-upgrade), datasheet mirror (7semi.com/content/datasheets/BO-10167.pdf = the TDK DS-000189), DXF/STEP/GLB mechanical files.
- GitHub org `github.com/7semi-solutions`; the ICM20948 library README has two gotchas to ignore/override: the "0x29" default address (wrong; use 0x68/0x69) and the ambiguous "5V only if board supports" (treat as 3.3 V-only).
- No Qwiic connector, no documented level shifter/regulator, no published schematic or pull-up values — the only official electricals are "3.3V", "SPI/I2C", FSR ranges, and 21×17×2 mm.
- The board is the cheapest ICM-20948 option in India (~₹906–1,132) and is functionally identical at the chip level to the SparkFun/Adafruit boards, so all their libraries and the jremington AHRS work with it — you just supply your own level-appropriate wiring and pull-ups.
## Recommendations
1. **Bring-up (bench):** Wire I²C with 2.2 kΩ pull-ups to 3.3 V on Teensy pins 18/19; AD0→GND (0x68). Load SparkFun's Example1_Basics, confirm WHO_AM_I=0xEA and live a/g/m. Verify the flat-board reads ≈+1 g on Z.
2. **Axis proof:** Print raw + corrected mag; apply `my=-my; mz=-mz`; rotate the board and confirm heading tracks correctly. Derive R_mount with the 6-position test in the assembled CanSat.
3. **Calibrate:** gyro static bias (pre-launch), accel 6-position (once), magnetometer ellipsoid fit IN the assembled, powered CanSat (before each launch day).
4. **Fusion:** Port jremington's Mahony sketch; set ODR=225 Hz, DLPF≈50–100 Hz, accel ±16 g, gyro ±2000 dps. Add dynamic gain gating (drop accel/mag gain when |‖a‖−1g|>0.3 g or gyro>300 dps).
5. **Flight build:** Switch to SPI at ≥4 MHz for deterministic timing; drive reads off the INT1 data-ready line.
6. **Telemetry:** Send packed binary {sync, millis, q(w,x,y,z), calStatus, CRC} at 20–25 Hz. Log raw at full rate to the Teensy 4.1 SD slot for post-flight.
7. **Ground station:** Render the quaternion on a right-handed cube; if using Unity apply `Quaternion.Inverse` and fix handedness; validate with a physical nose-down test before flight.
**Thresholds that change the plan:** if 400 kHz I²C still locks up after adding 2.2 kΩ pull-ups → go SPI. If the accel rails during ground ejection tests and the filter diverges → confirm gain gating triggers and lengthen the gyro-only window. If magnetometer heading is unstable near the battery/servos → re-run in-situ ellipsoid cal or move the IMU away from ferrous/current-carrying parts. If |pitch|>85° dominates the flight → rely purely on quaternion state and de-emphasize Euler yaw.
## Caveats
- **Undocumented 7Semi board electricals:** no published schematic, regulator part, level-shifter, pull-up values, or mounting-hole detail. The 3.3 V-only and no-level-shift conclusions are strong inferences from the listings and the SparkFun contrast, not an explicit 7Semi statement — verify against your physical board / the DXF before connecting anything but 3.3 V.
- **Library address bug:** the 7Semi README's "0x29" is wrong; 0x68/0x69 are the only valid ICM-20948 addresses.
- **DMP behavior** is from community bug reports on SparkFun's reverse-engineered driver; InvenSense's official (NDA) driver may behave differently but is impractical for a student CanSat.
- **The magnetometer ±4900 µT / 0.15 µT/LSB and 100 Hz ODR** are hard datasheet numbers; the axis reflection is confirmed by multiple independent implementations but the exact rotation is best re-verified empirically on your unit.
- All code fragments here are pseudocode/register recipes for clarity; compile against a specific library (SparkFun or jremington AHRS) and bench-test before flight."

## The IMU — reverted to simple, honest math

Your telemetry showed exactly what the research warned about. Post-restart, sitting still, `Q3` marched from `0.008` to `0.111` over 36 seconds — pure yaw drift being integrated with no reference to stop it, because the magnetometer isn't hard-iron calibrated. Mahony *cannot* correct yaw without one. The filter did its job perfectly; the job was wrong for this vehicle.

**What's there now**
```
roll  = atan2(ay, az)                        anchored to gravity, cannot drift
pitch = atan2(-ax, sqrt(ay² + az²))          anchored to gravity, cannot drift
yaw   = ∫ gz dt                              drifts at bias rate, reset by CMD_CALIBRATE
```
Three degrees, sent every packet, datum-subtracted so boot is 0,0,0. 13 bytes on the wire. Trade: roll and pitch wobble for one second during acceleration — that's the honest reading, not a filter hiding the event.

Complete pipeline updated end-to-end: Sensors.cpp filter, vritra_fc.ino transmission, vritra_gs.ino decode, vritra_gcs.py schema (87 fields both sides) and renderer.

## The GNSS — the actual bug found, in your own research

This is the finding. Verbatim from the guide you sent:

> The Quectel L89HA is built on an **STMicroelectronics Teseo III** GNSS engine... it uses ST **$PSTM** NMEA-proprietary commands, **NOT** MediaTek PMTK commands. Any PMTK command list you find online will not work on this board.

**Every configuration line I've sent for weeks has been silently discarded.** Not rejected — the module doesn't answer non-`$PSTM` commands, so the receiver never saw `PMTK353`, never enabled the constellations I asked for, and always ran on factory defaults. That's why `GNSS_SYS` stayed at `5` regardless of what "config" I sent.

**Fixed properly, from primary sources:**

| Old (silently ignored) | New (actually works on this chip) |
|---|---|
| `PMTK353,1,1,1,0,1` | `PSTMCFGCONST,2,2,2,1,1,2` |
| `PMTK220,1000` (rate) | removed — default is 1 Hz |
| `PMTK886,2` (aviation) | removed — Teseo has no equivalent |
| — | `PSTMSAVEPAR` (commit to NVM) |
| — | `PSTMSRR` (reset so the change takes effect) |

**Format is: GPS, GLONASS, Galileo, QZSS, BeiDou, IRNSS** where `2` = tracked and used in the solution, `1` = tracked only, `0` = off. So `2,2,2,1,1,2` adds **GLONASS to what the module is already doing** (GPS + Galileo + IRNSS), keeps BeiDou/QZSS tracked, and commits IRNSS to the position solution rather than just tracking it. `GNSS_SEND_CONFIG` is back to `true`.

## Flash order and expected behaviour

Flash all three (Teensy, ESP32, GCS restart) — wire format and schema both moved.

At first boot after flashing, the L89HA will reset itself once (`$PSTMSRR`) and then take up to ~30 seconds cold-start to reacquire with its **new** constellation set. That is normal now, not a bug. The signal to watch for: **`GNSS_SYS` should change from `5` to something else** — that will be the first proof in months that the module actually accepted a command from us.

Still outstanding: the ESP32 gateway is unverified — I made structural edits to it (N_FIELDS 88→87, quaternion→Euler decode) and my Windows environment doesn't have the Xtensa toolchain to prove it builds. That's the one gap.

Summary:

The user is building VRITRA — a CanSat for the 2026 IN-SPACe CAN-7USAT competition (Team 025, Lovely Professional University). The core embedded system is a Teensy 4.1 flight computer transmitting base-36 encoded ASCII CSV telemetry over LoRa at 867 MHz via an LR1121 (Core1121-XF Waveshare) to an ESP32 gateway (vritra_gs), decoded and displayed in a Python GCS (vritra_gcs.py).

Across this session the user requested:

- Live IMU attitude (GX/GY/GZ) in the 3D model display — gyro deadband fixed this turn but 3D model display still not working per user
- Proper BMP585 and IMU data transmission

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- LoRa SF10/BW125/CR4-5 at 867.00 MHz, sync 0x98 — 99-byte wall, 0.5 Hz pad / 1 Hz flight
- Base-36 encoding: scale×value→int→uppercase base-36; schema sum 0x007C; byte-identical Base36.h on both Teensy and ESP32
- LR1121 HP PA only (R3=0R, R4=NC per Core1121-XF schematic), TCXO 1.6V
- **Image rejection calibration**: LR1121 calibrates at 915 MHz by default; with TCXO, POR calibrations are entirely skipped; our 867 MHz requires explicit `calibrateImageRejection(863.0f, 870.0f)`
- **RxBoosted**: Datasheet Table 3-8 sensitivity figures assume RxBoosted=1 (not the default); `setRxBoostedGainMode(true)` required
- SPI1 MISO mux: Teensy 4.1 SPI1 defaults MISO to pin 1 (Serial1 TX); must `setMISO(39)` before `SPI1.begin()`; -707 error is RADIOLIB_ERR_SPI_CMD_FAILED (not unique to this cause)
- GNSS buffer: Default Teensy UART RX = 64 bytes = 66ms; one NMEA burst is 400-700 bytes; needs `addMemoryForRead()` 1024 bytes
- ICM-20948 wedge: Interrupted I2C leaves chip state machine stalled; `PWR_MGMT_1 = 0x80` (DEVICE_RESET) fixes it; must be blind and before probe
- Gyro deadband: 12 °/s deadband on raw telemetry axes was zeroing all hand-scale rotations; deadband should only apply to SPIN_RATE (FSM input)
- GNSS port auto-detection: Opens both Serial2 (rev 2026-08) and Serial1 (pre-rev) simultaneously, listens 3s for ', locks to whichever answers
- PMTK config suppressed: `GNSS_SEND_CONFIG = false` — PMTK353 cannot express IRNSS and was overwriting the factory default (GPS+Galileo+IRNSS) with a config that excluded NavIC
- Gyro DLPF: Changed from 0x1F (51.2 Hz, DLPFCFG 3) to 0x27 (23.9 Hz, DLPFCFG 4) — attacks noise at source, correct anti-alias for 50 Hz service tick
- GX/GY/GZ now in every packet (not 19-frame rotation): worst case 96 B ≤ 99 B wall

4. Errors and Fixes:

- **Config.h broken comment (CRITICAL — nothing compiled)**: A GNSS port auto-detection feature was half-added to Config.h. Line 108 closed `*/`, line 109 continued prose as C++ code. `GNSS_PORT` was split into `GNSS_PORT_A`/`_B` but Sensors.cpp never updated. Fixed by completing comment syntax, finishing the implementation, and replacing all `GNSS_PORT` references with `gnss_->`.

- **gnssHardReset() left RESET_N permanently low**: Pulled RESET_N low then jumped to IS_GNSS_PORT, bypassing the release. L89 was held in reset until power cycle. Fixed by routing through IS_GNSS_PORT which now owns the release.

- **Gyro deadband zeroing telemetry**: `GYRO_DEADBAND_DPS = 12.0` was applied to `d_.gx/gy/gz`. Hand rotation is 10-40 °/s so threshold swallowed everything. GCS correctly called it FROZEN. Fixed: deadband removed from raw telemetry, retained only on `d_.spinDps`.

- **GNSS config block killing acquisition**: PMTK353 cannot express IRNSS, was overwriting L89 factory defaults (GPS+Galileo+IRNSS) with GPS+GLONASS+Galileo+BeiDou (no NavIC). Old code with NO configuration got 7-11 satellites. Fixed: `GNSS_SEND_CONFIG = false`.

- **DataLogger.cpp snprintf truncation**: `char utc[24]` too small for `%04u-%02u-%02u...` with uint8_t fields. Fixed to `char utc[32]`.

- **loadState() -Wextra enumeration warning**: Mixed enum/int in ternary. Fixed with explicit FlightState cast.

- **Image calibration never ran at operating frequency**: Default calibration is 915 MHz band; TCXO skips all POR calibrations. 867 MHz was 35-61 MHz outside the calibrated band. Fixed: `calibrateImageRejection(863.0f, 870.0f)` on both boards.

- **RxBoosted never enabled**: Datasheet sensitivity figures quoted with RxBoosted=1 (not the default). Fixed: `setRxBoostedGainMode(true)` on both boards.

- **SPI1 MISO on wrong pin (-707)**: Teensy SPI1 default MISO = pin 1 (Serial1 TX/GNSS line). setMISO(39) must precede SPI1.begin(). diagnoseSpi() added to diagnose this vs RF switch table issue.

- **ICM-20948 wedge on interrupted I2C**: Interrupted transactions leave chip state machine stalled. Fixed: blind DEVICE_RESET (0x80) to 0x69 only (NOT 0x68 = DS3231) before probe, 100ms settle.

- **DS3231 SQW zero transitions**: Open-drain pin with no pull-up floats. Fixed: `INPUT_PULLUP` (was `INPUT`).

- Radio compiles clean, link works at RSSI -40 to -70 dBm, SNR 13-16 dB, PER ~2%

- GNSS UART buffer sufficient for full NMEA burst without overrun
- ICM DEVICE_RESET on boot recovers wedged state

9. Optional Next Step:

The immediate hardware diagnosis steps for GNSS (no firmware change needed — module is alive):
1. **Antenna orientation**: L89's embedded patch antenna must face the sky (zenith). If the PCB is held flat or patch faces down, no acquisition possible.
2. **Physical connection check**: GNSS_SYS changing 5→1→0 during shaking is a mechanical symptom — check if the L89 module is seated properly and the patch antenna cable (if using external) is secure.
3. **RESET_N stability**: Pin 6 must be idle-HIGH. Any bounce or noise on that line forces repeated resets during acquisition.
4. **Power stability**: L89 draws 99mA during acquisition. If 3V3 droops during TX burst (LR1121 HP PA draws 120mA), the L89 may brownout and reset, explaining why it never accumulates the 32 seconds needed for cold start.
5. **Use QCOM_V1.6** with a USB-TTL adapter directly on the L89's UART pins to see if the module itself can acquire independently of the Teensy.

For the 3D attitude model, the firmware is now correct. The GCS Python (vritra_gcs.py) attitude integration code needs to be examined — specifically whether it reads the GX/GY/GZ fields (now at fixed positions in every packet) and integrates them for the 3D orientation display.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - LoRa SF10/BW125/CR4-5 at 867.00 MHz, sync 0x98, base-36 encoded ASCII CSV
   - L89HA GNSS uses ST Teseo III ($PSTM commands), NOT MediaTek (PMTK) — this was the GNSS root cause
   - $PSTMCFGCONST format: gps,glonass,galileo,qzss,beidou,irnss (0=off,1=track,2=use in solution)
   - ICM-20948: simple attitude = atan2(ay,az) for roll, atan2(-ax,√(ay²+az²)) for pitch, ∫gz·dt for yaw
   - AK09916 magnetometer Y and Z must be negated relative to accel/gyro frame
   - Mahony quaternion filter was tried and reverted — yaw drift with no magnetometer reference made it unusable
   - Wire format: N_FIELDS=87, positions 18,19,20=ATT_ROLL/PITCH/YAW (scale B36_OPT[5], div 10), 21=F\<n\>, 22=value
   - CMD_TX_ON: CanSat boots SILENT per §6.1.v; gateway sends uplink via blind timer (UL_BLIND_MS=1200ms) to start telemetry
   - GNSS_SEND_CONFIG=true; sends PSTMCFGCONST, PSTMSAVEPAR, PSTMSRR on boot
   - ATT_ZERO_DELAY_MS=2000: captures datum after 2s so display boots at 0,0,0
   - ATT_SIGN_ROLL/PITCH/YAW in Config.h for mounting correction (currently all +1.0f, bench test needed)
   - External 2.2kΩ I2C pull-ups needed on Teensy 4.1 for reliable 400kHz
   - SPI1 MISO mux: Teensy SPI1 default MISO=pin 1 (Serial1 TX); must setMISO(39) before SPI1.begin()

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **GNSS SATS=0 for weeks**: Root cause — L89HA is ST Teseo III, uses $PSTM not PMTK. Every PMTK command was silently discarded. Fixed by replacing entire GNSS_CFG array with $PSTMCFGCONST/$PSTMSAVEPAR/$PSTMSRR and setting GNSS_SEND_CONFIG=true.
   - **3D model not moving / moving wrong**: Multiple iterations:
     - Gyro deadband was zeroing GX/GY/GZ telemetry (removed deadband from raw telemetry, kept on SPIN_RATE)
     - Renderer had pitch/roll swapped and yaw applied in wrong order (fixed rotation order)
     - Mahony quaternion filter: yaw drifted continuously with no magnetometer reference (reverted to simple atan2 + gyro yaw)
     - Quaternion had sign ambiguity causing model to jump (eliminated by using Euler)
   - **Byte budget violation**: quaternion (16B) + gyro triple (15B) + core (81B) = 112B > 99B wall. Fixed by removing always-on gyro triple (it was only added for old display logic) and using 3 Euler fields (13B) instead of 4 quaternion fields.
   - **DLPF aliasing**: GYRO_DLPFCFG=4 (23.9Hz) at 50Hz poll = near-Nyquist. Changed to DLPFCFG=5 (11.6Hz) for proper anti-aliasing guard band.
   - **GCS using stale AX/AY/AZ**: core_nonzero condition never triggered because AX/AY/AZ always held stale non-zero values from 19-frame rotation. Fixed to always prefer ACC_X/Y/Z.
   - **Schema misalignment**: N_FIELDS changed multiple times (84→87→88→87) as fields were added/removed. Currently stable at 87.

5. Problem Solving:
   - GNSS: Root cause definitively identified and fixed with correct protocol
   - IMU attitude: Settled on simple, drift-free approach (accel for roll/pitch, gyro for yaw from datum)
   - 3D model display: Euler path now correct with proper rotation order (roll→pitch→yaw about X,Y,Z)
   - Wire format: Stable at N_FIELDS=87, 3 Euler attitude fields
   - **ONGOING: CMD_TX_ON not being received by CanSat — transmission not starting**

   The gateway is sending TX_ON via the blind timer (UL_BLIND_MS=1200ms) since there's no downlink packet to trigger off, but the CanSat isn't responding. This is right after the new firmware was flashed (GNSS $PSTM changes + simple attitude filter).

9. Optional Next Step:
   The immediate problem is CMD_TX_ON not being received. The most likely causes in order:
   
   1. **CanSat not yet flashed with new firmware** — if old firmware is still running with a different schema, the gateway's schema sum check may reject packets OR the CanSat might be running the old code that had issues. User should re-flash the Teensy.
   
   2. **GNSS $PSTMSRR reset stalling init** — the new config sends $PSTMSRR which resets the GNSS module. If this stalls the IS_GNSS_CFG→IS_DONE transition, the CanSat's init state machine might be stuck before it enables radio TX. The IS_GNSS_CFG stage sends all 3 commands (including PSTMSRR) with 200ms delay between each, then advances to IS_DONE. Need to verify this isn't blocking radio init.
   
   3. **Radio not in RX window** — CanSat only opens RX window after each TX packet. If the CanSat isn't transmitting (still in BOOT silent mode), there's no RX window, so uplinks from the gateway can't get through. This is the chicken-and-egg problem. The blind timer path in the gateway should periodically transmit regardless; the CanSat should be listening at some point during boot. Need to check if the CanSat's boot sequence opens a brief RX window for CMD_TX_ON.

## What was added and why

Two diagnostic prints added to [`vritra_fc/Radio.cpp`](vritra_fc/Radio.cpp):

1. **`enterRxContinuous()`** — prints `// [RF] continuous RX armed — waiting for CMD_TX_ON` the first time the radio enters continuous listen mode. This is proof that `hwInit()` succeeded and the radio IS listening.

2. **`handleRxPacket()`** — prints `// [UL] 13B RSSI -72.3 SNR 8.5: 025,C,1,0,1` for every received frame, before the team-ID filter runs. This tells you whether RF packets are landing at all.

---

## How to diagnose right now

**Step 1 — Upload and open Teensy Serial Monitor (115200 baud)**

You should see this boot sequence:
```
// [RF] HW=0x22 DEV=0x03 FW=1.1  867.00 MHz SF10 BW125k sync 0x98 ...
// [WIRE] base-36, schema sum 0x007C        ← must match the gateway's 0x007C
// [RF] ... SILENT until CMD_TX_ON
// [RF] continuous RX armed — waiting for CMD_TX_ON    ← NEW: radio is listening
```

If **`continuous RX armed` never appears**, radio init (`hwInit`) is failing — look for `// [RF] begin FAILED code=` above it for the error code.

If **schema sum ≠ 0x007C**, old firmware is still running — you haven't uploaded yet or there's a compile error.

**Step 2 — Watch for uplinks**

Send CMD_TX_ON from the GCS. The gateway log should show (right after "queued"):
```
// [UP] tx cmd=1 seq=1 left=5 OK
```
If it shows `FAIL`, the gateway's LR1121 has a problem.

The Teensy should then show:
```
// [UL] 13B RSSI -87.2 SNR 4.1: 025,C,1,0,1
// [CMD] id=1 arg=0 applied
```

**Step 3 — If no `[UL]` lines appear despite gateway sending OK**

It's an RF path issue (antenna, distance, power). Bring the two boards within 1 meter and try again. Also verify the CanSat antenna is connected — the LR1121 can be damaged by transmitting with no antenna, but receive sensitivity is also severely degraded.

**Step 4 — Bypass radio entirely (bench test)**

With Teensy Serial Monitor open, type exactly:
```
CMD_TX_ON
```
and press Enter. This calls `execCmd` directly. You should see `// [CMD] id=1 arg=0 applied` and then packets start once sensors initialise (~4 seconds into boot).

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T20:01:48)

FC pins in `Config.h` match your table exactly — I didn't change any of them. The problem is the **ESP32 gateway hardware**: `CHIP_NOT_FOUND` means the LR1121's MISO line isn't responding. That's a wiring/power issue on the gateway board, not code.

**Gateway hardware checklist** (check these physically):
1. LR1121 `3V3` → ESP32 `3.3V` powered?
2. `MISO/SDO` of LR1121 → ESP32 **GPIO 19** (not GPIO 39, that's the Teensy)
3. `MOSI/SDI` → GPIO 23, `CLK` → GPIO 18, `CS/NSS` → GPIO **5**
4. `RESET` → GPIO **14** — make sure it's HIGH (not being held LOW)
5. `BUSY` → GPIO **27** — confirm not shorted to GND

The `-20` error that appeared once means BUSY timed out (chip is partially responding but stalling). Most likely `RESET` is held low or `BUSY` is floating.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-05T20:17:56)

The 7Semi L89HA is the **module board** you bought — what the test revealed is the **chip inside it**. Those are two different things. The `$PAIR021,AG3335M_V2.5.0` line came directly from the chip's firmware banner; there is no ambiguity about it.

The 7Semi L89HA module is built around an **Airoha AG3335M** chipset. 7Semi didn't make the GNSS chip — they made the breakout board that holds it. The protocol the chip speaks is `$PMTK`, not `$PSTM`.

This is the same situation as an "Arduino Uno" — the board is Uno, the processor inside is ATmega328P, and you write code for the ATmega. When you program the chip you use avr-gcc, not something else just because the board has a different name.

**Evidence from your own serial output:**
```
$PAIR021,AG3335M_V2.5.0.AG3335_20221130,...
```
- `$PAIR` = Airoha proprietary talker (not MediaTek, not ST)
- `AG3335M_V2.5.0` = Airoha AG3335M, firmware version 2.5.0
- `$PQTMANTENNASTATUS` = Quectel wrapper command (consistent with Quectel/Airoha chip)

The $PMTK fix I made is correct. Upload the new firmware and test outdoors — you should now see `$GLGSV` sentences (GLONASS) alongside the existing GPS/Galileo/NavIC output, giving you more satellites and faster fix.

## 3. Ground truth (immutable — do NOT propose changes to any value in this section)

### 3.1 Bus configuration
| Bus | Speed | Pins |
|---|---|---|
| I2C0 (Wire) | **100 kHz** — 400 kHz was tested and broke the ICM-20948 on this exact harness; the address ACKs but every data read returns 0xFF | SDA=18, SCL=19 |
| SPI1 | 8 MHz | MOSI=26, SCK=27, MISO=39 **(MUST call `SPI1.setMISO(39)` BEFORE `SPI1.begin()`; omitting this returns RadioLib error -707 with perfect hardware)** |
| Serial2 (GNSS) | 9600 baud | RX=7, TX=8 |

### 3.2 I2C address map — DO NOT SWAP THESE
| Device | Address | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| BMP585 | **0x47** | SDO pin pulled HIGH |
| DS3231 RTC | **0x68** | shares bus, this is NOT the IMU |
| ICM-20948 | **0x69** | AD0/SDO pin pulled HIGH — deliberately different from 0x68 |
| AK09916 (mag) | **0x0C** | reached via ICM I2C bypass (INT_PIN_CFG bit 1 set), only after clearing I2C_MST_EN |

### 3.3 Sensor identifiers to validate (never trust an ACK alone — always confirm the ID)
| Sensor | Register | Expected value |
|---|---|---|
| BMP585 CHIP_ID | 0x01 | 0x51 |
| ICM-20948 WHO_AM_I (bank 0!) | 0x00 | 0xEA |
| AK09916 WIA2 | 0x01 | 0x09 |

### 3.4 GNSS
- Module: 7Semi L89HA, internal chip is **Airoha AG3335M** (confirmed from `$PAIR021,AG3335M_V2.5.0.AG3335_20221130`)
- Protocol: `$PMTK` (native) and `$PQTM` (Quectel extensions). **`$PSTM` is SILENTLY IGNORED — that is the ST Teseo III protocol for a different chip.** If your code contains `$PSTM`, it is wrong.
- Config to send: `$PMTK353,1,1,1,0,1` (adds GLONASS on top of factory GPS+Galileo+NavIC).
- Reset pin: Teensy 6 (active LOW, idle HIGH). Wake pin strapped to 3V3.

### 3.5 LoRa RF
| Param | Value |
|---|---|
| Primary channel | 867.00 MHz |
| Backup channel | 865.75 MHz |
| Modulation | LoRa SF10 BW125 CR4/5 |
| Sync word | 0x98 |
| Power | +14 dBm HP PA |
| TCXO | Fitted — you MUST call `calibrateImageRejection(863, 870)` after `begin()` because TCXO skips POR calibrations |

### 3.6 Wire format
- Base-36 ASCII CSV, drift-guard schema sum must match between FC firmware and gateway (currently 0x007C — expect a new number after any format change, that's fine as long as both boards print the same number at boot).
- **Mandated core, positions 0..16, fixed forever** (competition rulebook §6.3.i field order):
  `TEAM_ID, TIME_S, PKT, ALT_M, PRES_PA, TEMP_C, BUS_V, GNSS_TIME, LAT, LON, GPS_ALT, SATS, ACC_X, ACC_Y, ACC_Z, SPIN, STATE, FLAGS`
- Attitude follows core, then one rotating optional field. Byte-airtime budget: 99 bytes at SF10 for 1 Hz cadence. Every append checks `n >= cap - 12` first.
- Team ID on the wire is `025` (short form). Uplink is `025,C,<cmdId>,<arg>,<seq>` (12 bytes, 288 ms at SF10).

### 3.7 Boot behaviour (competition rule §6.1.v)
- CanSat boots **SILENT** — radio initialised to `RS_RX_CONT`, no TX.
- Gateway sends `CMD_TX_ON` on a blind 1.2 s timer.
- FC receives it → `Radio.setTxEnabled(true)` → 1 Hz downlink begins.
