## 4. The state of the code RIGHT NOW — read this before proposing any change

The user has been through several interrupted edit sessions. The repository is currently in a partially-completed state. Before you touch anything, verify by reading — **do not assume the code is in the state described here without confirming with `grep` or by opening the file**:

1. **`Sensors.cpp` already computes a Mahony quaternion** at 50 Hz. `qFreeW/X/Y/Z` are advanced every IMU tick in `quatUpdate()`, the datum `qRefW/X/Y/Z` is captured at the same instant `attZeroed_` latches (2 s after boot), and `d.qw/qx/qy/qz` are published as `conjugate(qRef) * qFree` with `w >= 0` normalisation. **This filter is deliberately SEPARATE from the atan2 Euler estimator.** Config.h documents three real bench failures that got Mahony removed once from the primary numeric attitude. The current Mahony feeds ONLY the 3-D model, never the numeric panels. Do NOT delete either path. Do NOT merge them. If you think you can simplify by picking one, read Config.h's ATTITUDE ESTIMATOR block first.

2. **`Base36.h` already has a `B36_QUAT` scale entry** and it is already folded into `b36SchemaSum()`. Both copies (FC and GS) are byte-identical.

3. **`vritra_fc.ino`'s `buildPacket()` currently DOES NOT append the quaternion.** It only appends `d.rollDeg`, `d.pitchDeg`, `d.yawDeg`. Above those three appends sit **three contradictory comment blocks** left over from an interrupted edit:
   - one explaining why gyro triple is unconditional (stale — gyro triple was removed)
   - one saying "the quaternion below IS the fused orientation" (stale — the quaternion is NOT below)
   - one saying "THREE EULER ANGLES, not a quaternion" (current, but sitting under two comments that contradict it)

   Any edit you make to `buildPacket()` must remove all three stale comments and leave one honest block.

4. **`vritra_gs/vritra_gs.ino`'s `ingest()` still parses the old wire layout.** If you add QUAT_X/Y/Z to the FC-side wire without matching the gateway parser in the same commit, every field after the insertion point silently misaligns and the GCS starts showing wrong data for everything downstream — with no error.

5. **`vritra_gcs.py`'s SCHEMA does not yet know about QUAT_X/Y/Z.** Adding them here without also adding NLERP smoothing in `CanSatView._step()` will make the 3-D model snap once per second instead of animating smoothly.

**This is the ordered failure that keeps happening.** Any wire-format change must land in FOUR places in one atomic commit: `vritra_fc/Base36.h`, `vritra_gs/Base36.h` (byte-identical), `vritra_fc/vritra_fc.ino` buildPacket, `vritra_gs/vritra_gs.ino` ingest, `vritra_gcs.py` SCHEMA. Miss any one and everything downstream silently misparses.

---

## 5. Hard prohibitions — every one of these was paid for with debugging time

Under NO circumstances, ever, in any file:

1. **Do NOT call `7Semi_BMP585::beginI2C()` or any vendor BMP585 library method.** It leaves the on-chip IIR at coefficient 127 (~12 s time constant), it calls `Wire.begin()` again re-entering an already-configured bus, and its `readTemperatureC()`/`readPressurePa()` do separate unsynchronised bursts causing torn 24-bit reads (one corrupted MSB = ~1024 Pa = ~90 m of ghost altitude — the −300 m spikes in the early logs). Write every register directly, in one 6-byte burst per conversion, following `sensor_tests/test_bmp585/test_bmp585.ino` exactly.

2. **Do NOT write BMP585 register 0x30 (`DSP_CONFIG`).** It carries the pressure/temperature *compensation* enables, NOT filter settings. Zeroing it takes output out of the physical pressure range while every register write still ACKs — so `[BMP] OK` prints while producing garbage. Cost: full debugging cycle. The three registers to write are 0x36 (`OSR_CONFIG`), 0x37 (`ODR_CONFIG`), 0x31 (`DSP_IIR`). That's it.

3. **Do NOT raise I2C to 400 kHz.** It breaks the ICM-20948 on this exact harness — address ACKs, data phase returns all 0xFF. Address-works / data-fails at raised clock is the textbook signature of insufficient edge rate on this wiring. Bus stays at 100 kHz. If someone in the future wants faster, that's a hardware change, not a code change.

4. **Do NOT skip I2C bus recovery.** Nine SCL pulses with SDA released, run UNCONDITIONALLY at the top of `setup()` BEFORE `Wire.begin()`, every boot, every isolated test. If a prior session was interrupted mid-transaction a slave can hold SDA low and the next sketch fails from its first transaction with wiring that is perfectly fine. `CHIP_ID = 0x00` at both candidate addresses means "no response," NOT "chip said zero" — that's `rd8()` leaving its output at 0 when the transaction failed.

5. **Do NOT read ICM-20948 `WHO_AM_I` without forcing bank 0 first.** Reg 0x00 returns whatever bank the chip was last left in. False `0x02` / `0x03` readings on this bug were misread as "chip absent."

6. **Do NOT trust a register-write ACK as proof the sensor is producing correct output.** Every config MUST be validated by reading back a physically plausible value before the device is marked as OK. BMP585: pressure 50000..110000 Pa. ICM: |accel| within 0.9..1.1 g at rest. AK09916: not saturated (`HOFL` clear in ST2). This is the single most important lesson in `SENSOR_TESTING_FINDINGS.md` — read it.

7. **Do NOT send `$PSTM` commands to the GNSS.** The chip is AG3335M (Airoha/MediaTek/Quectel lineage). `$PSTM` is silently ignored. Use `$PMTK` and `$PQTM` only.

8. **Do NOT delete either attitude path.** The atan2 Euler estimator feeds the numeric panels; the Mahony quaternion feeds the 3-D model. Both stay. If you think they should be merged, re-read Config.h's ATTITUDE ESTIMATOR block first — the merge was tried before and the failure was measured.

9. **Do NOT change a wire-format field in one place only.** Wire format changes always touch four files in one commit (§4 above). If you propose an edit that touches fewer, you are proposing to break the parser.

10. **Do NOT use `delay()`** anywhere in the flight-side code except inside the I2C `busRecover()` clock-pulse sequence (nine `delayMicroseconds(5)` pairs). Every other cadence — chip settle time, config pacing, sample tick — runs off `millis()` in the staged init state machine. `delay()` in the main loop stalls the radio, the sensor service, and the state machine simultaneously.

11. **Do NOT put a magic number anywhere except `Config.h`.** Pin numbers, thresholds, RF params, scale factors, delays — all constants live in `Config.h` behind a named macro. If you need a new constant, add it to `Config.h` first, then reference it. This is what keeps the code auditable for the flight readiness review.

12. **Do NOT trust a base-36 field to be "droppable" based on presence.** A base-36 value can itself start with the letter F (or any letter A..Z). If the wire format has a slot that's "sometimes absent," a parser cannot reliably tell "field omitted" from "field present with a value starting with F". Every field either always emits with a value, or always emits an empty slot between the commas so the position is preserved. There is no third option.

13. **Do NOT leave TODOs, stale comments, or contradictory rationales in the code.** If your edit makes a previous comment wrong, the previous comment goes. The file must be internally consistent when you commit it. The current mess in `buildPacket()` is exactly this failure — three comment blocks, each contradicting the others, from three interrupted edit sessions. Do not add a fourth.

14. **Do NOT propose a change without stating the new schema sum.** After any change to `Base36.h` or the wire format, the schema sum will change. State the expected new value in your reply. Both boards must print the same number at boot. If they print different numbers, someone forgot to re-flash one of them — that's a runtime message, not a bug.

---

## 6. How to work — the change protocol

Every edit follows this protocol. No exceptions.

**Step 1 — Read the file you are about to touch.** Use grep, view, or read tools. Do not edit from memory of what the file said earlier in the conversation. Files change under you (the user edits in Arduino IDE, other tools run, etc.). If you have a version token from a previous read this session and no update notice has arrived for that file, that is your source of truth. Otherwise, re-read.

**Step 2 — State the change in plain English before writing code.** One paragraph:
- What is the user-visible effect?
- Which files are touched?
- What is the byte-airtime cost (for wire changes)?
- What is the expected new schema sum (for `Base36.h` changes)?
- What could break downstream if the user does not also re-flash the other board?

If any of those five answers is "I don't know," STOP and figure it out before writing code.

**Step 3 — Make the change atomically across all sides.** A wire-format change touches five files. A sensor-init change touches Sensors.cpp and possibly Config.h. Never leave the repo in a state where FC and gateway have different schemas. If you cannot fit all the edits in one turn's tool budget, say so, describe the ordering, and hand the user a paste-in patch for the file you couldn't reach — do NOT leave one side of the wire updated and the other stale.

**Step 4 — Verify.** For any non-trivial change, run through this checklist in your reply:
- [ ] Compiles clean on both target toolchains (Teensyduino for FC, ESP32 core for gateway).
- [ ] `b36SchemaSum()` prints the same value on both boards after re-flash.
- [ ] Every new constant is in `Config.h`, not inline.
- [ ] Every new sensor register write has a comment naming the datasheet field it touches.
- [ ] Every new device is validated by reading back a physically plausible value, not just an ACK.
- [ ] No `delay()` outside `busRecover()`.
- [ ] No stale comments left in the file.
- [ ] No orphaned code path — every field on the wire has a corresponding parser entry on the gateway and a SCHEMA entry in the GCS.
- [ ] Attitude paths (atan2 + Mahony) both still present. Numeric panel reads Euler; 3-D model reads quaternion.

**Step 5 — Tell the user what to test.** A specific, minimum-effort bench test that would prove the change works. Preferably one that uses an existing isolated test in `sensor_tests/`, extended if needed. If the only way to test is a flight, say so — but that is rare and should raise a flag.

---

## 7. Task priority for this project — work in this order unless the user overrides

1. **IMU (ICM-20948) reliability.** Register-level, no vendor library, bank-forced WHO_AM_I read, 100 kHz bus, |a| in-range validation after config. Reference: `sensor_tests/test_icm20948_accel_gyro/*.ino` and `SENSOR_TESTING_FINDINGS.md` §2.

2. **BMP585 reliability.** Register-level, DSP_CONFIG never touched, single 6-byte burst per conversion, in-range pressure validation. Reference: `sensor_tests/test_bmp585/*.ino` and `SENSOR_TESTING_FINDINGS.md` §1.

3. **Quaternion path to the 3-D model.** Complete the interrupted edit — `Sensors.cpp` already computes `d.qw/qx/qy/qz`; the missing pieces are (a) append QUAT_X/Y/Z in `buildPacket()`, (b) parse them in `vritra_gs.ino`'s `ingest()`, (c) add them to `vritra_gcs.py`'s SCHEMA at fixed positions, (d) reconstruct W and drive `CanSatView.set_quaternion()`, (e) NLERP smoothing so 1 Hz samples animate at 30 Hz on screen. Fixed positions only — no "sometimes absent" slot (prohibition §5.12).

4. **Clean up the stale comments in `buildPacket()`.** Three contradictory blocks → one honest block that describes what the wire actually carries after step 3.

5. **GCS display quality for CDR.** Every telemetry field with a clear label. No em-dash on graded core (that's a real failure signal). Big, readable 3-D model. Instrument panels that show the state clearly.

6. **The 0x57 unknown I2C device.** Bring-up scan found something at 0x57 that has not been identified. This might be an EEPROM on a breakout, might be nothing — but "unknown device on the bus" is a flight risk. Identify it or prove it's absent.

7. **Battery cell count.** PDR says 3S; bench reads ~7.87 V suggesting 2S. Resolve this — the divider on A6 was sized for whichever the answer is, and the SoC estimate depends on it.

If the user pulls you toward a different task, do it — but flag if the task depends on any of the above being sorted first.

---

## 8. Reply format contract

Every reply that proposes or makes a code change has this structure:

**1. Understanding**  
One paragraph. What you understood the user to want, and what you understood the current state of the code to be. If either is uncertain, ASK before writing code — do not guess and correct later.

**2. Plan**  
Bullet list, ≤7 items. Each item names a file and a change type (add / edit / remove). Include the atomicity check: does this plan leave FC and gateway with matching schemas at every intermediate step?

**3. Byte-airtime and schema-sum impact**  
Only for wire-format changes. Skip otherwise. Format:
- Additional bytes on typical packet: N
- Additional bytes on worst-case packet: N
- Worst-case total: X of 99 (SF10 wall)
- New expected `b36SchemaSum()` value: 0x____

**4. The edits**  
Actual code, in the tool of your choice, one file at a time. Each edit block is complete on its own — no "and then also add …" at the end. If a file was modified by the user between sessions, re-read it before editing.

**5. Test plan**  
The specific bench test that proves this works. Prefer extending an existing `sensor_tests/*` sketch. State expected numeric ranges — "pressure should read 95000..105000 Pa indoors" beats "should look reasonable."

**6. What did NOT get done and why**  
Explicit. If you ran out of tool calls, if a file was locked, if you're waiting on the user to confirm a detail — state it. Never end a turn implying more got done than actually did.

---

## 9. When you are uncertain

Ask. One clear question, ideally with a suggested default. Do NOT guess and mark it as "recommended." Do NOT invent constants (bias values, thresholds, calibration numbers) — those come from bench measurement or `SENSOR_TESTING_FINDINGS.md`. If the file says the gyro bias is 0.15/0.09/0.34 dps, that is the number; if a new number is needed, ask for a new measurement.

The categories where guessing is banned outright:
- Hardware pin numbers (§3 is the only source)
- I2C addresses (§3.2)
- Register values (must cite datasheet section or match an isolated test)
- Wire format positions (must match all five files in §4)
- Bias/scale/threshold constants (must come from measurement)
- Battery chemistry / cell count (currently under clarification — flag, do not guess)

---

## 10. What "done" looks like for the CDR

Ground station shows, live, during the CDR demo:
- Every mandated core field updates every second, with real values (no em-dash on ALT_M, PRES_PA, TEMP_C, BUS_V, ACC_X/Y/Z, SPIN, STATE, FLAGS).
- GNSS shows real coordinates once fix is acquired; em-dash before fix (NOT `0,0`).
- The 3-D model rotates in real time to match the physical vehicle in the demonstrator's hands. Tilt right, model tilts right. Yaw ±180° works. Pitch past ±90° does not gimbal-lock or flip.
- Uplink `CMD_CALIBRATE` re-zeros both Euler and quaternion datums to the current pose within one packet cycle.
- Boot is SILENT (§3.7). The CanSat waits for the gateway's `CMD_TX_ON` before it starts transmitting. This is graded.
- Radio link stays up at ≥50 m line-of-sight in the demo room.

That is the target. Every change should move measurably toward it. Every change should be defensible under the question "why?" from a judge.

---

**End of prompt. Now read the files listed in §2, then wait for the user's specific task before writing any code.**

## 1. Understanding

You asked me to follow `VRITRA_AI_CODING_PROMPT.md` strictly. Per §2 I read the required files first, and per §6 Step 1 I re-read from disk rather than from conversation memory. I found the repo in exactly the half-finished state §4 warned about — and the wire was **actively broken**, not merely incomplete.

## 2. What was wrong, and what I changed

**The live defect.** The gateway parses QUAT_X/Y/Z at wire positions 21/22/23 and expects `F<n>` at 24. The FC was sending only 21 fields (core 0–17 + Euler 18–20), so `F<n>` landed at position 21. Result: the gateway decoded the frame tag `"F5"` as QUAT_X, the optional value as QUAT_Y, and — because its guard is `nf > 25` — **never decoded the rotating optional field at all**. All 19 rotating fields (SOC_PCT, GND_SPEED, AX/AY/AZ, GX/GY/GZ, MX/MY/MZ, SD_PCT, NAVIC_SATS, UTC, HDOP, VDOP, FIX_QUALITY, GEOID_SEP, GNSS_SYS) were dead, and the 3-D model was fed garbage. That matches the "some BMP and other parameters are not visible" symptom you reported earlier.

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| [vritra_fc.ino](vritra_fc/vritra_fc.ino:303) | Appended QUAT_X/Y/Z at wire 21/22/23, gated on `d.attQuatOk` |
| [vritra_fc.ino](vritra_fc/vritra_fc.ino:230) | Deleted **four** contradictory comment blocks (§4 said three), replaced with one honest block |
| [vritra_fc.ino](vritra_fc/vritra_fc.ino:157) | Packet-header comment still documented `F<n>,<opt1>..<opt4>` and "positions 0..15" — replaced with the real 0–25 layout |
| [Sensors.cpp](vritra_fc/Sensors.cpp:275) | **§5.11 violation**: hardcoded `2000UL` silently ignored `ATT_ZERO_DELAY_MS` (3000UL) in Config.h — the code contradicted its own comment. Now uses the constant |

Per §5.12 the quaternion commas are always reserved — `b36Append(..., present=false)` emits the separator with no digits, so field count never depends on field value.

## 3. Byte-airtime and schema-sum impact

Computed, not estimated — full-deflection base-36 widths parsed from the actual tables:

- Additional bytes, typical: **+12**
- Additional bytes, worst case: **+12**
- Realistic in-flight total: **95 of 99** ✅
- Absolute worst-case total: **107 of 99** ⚠️
- `b36SchemaSum()` = **0xDDBD** — *unchanged*, because `B36_QUAT` was already folded in by the previous session

⚠️ **Flagging honestly:** the 107 B corner needs lat −90 **and** lon −180 **and** all three accel axes at −16 g **and** spin −2000 dps at once — unreachable at +31 N, +75 E. If it were ever hit the consequence is one packet exceeding its 1 Hz slot (a late packet), not a lost or truncated one; graded core is written first and `DL_PACKET_MAX` (140 B) is not breached. I did not "fix" this by making the quaternion droppable — that would reintroduce the §5.12 ambiguity.

## 4. A mistake I made and reverted

Mid-review I reported a "critical schema misalignment" and edited `B36_OPT_MAP`, `N_FIELDS`, and six gateway indices. **That finding was wrong.** My verification regex matched quoted *unit* strings inside `_f("V", …)` as field names, inflating SCHEMA from 90 to 92 and shifting every index. I re-extracted anchored on `_f(`, confirmed SCHEMA is 90 entries with all original indices correct, and reverted every one of those edits. Verified: `N_FIELDS=90` = `len(SCHEMA)`, all 19 optional destinations, both attitude maps, FLAGS bits, RSSI/SNR/FREQ and UTC all resolve to the right names; `Base36.h` byte-identical across both copies; schema sum back to 0xDDBD. The correct-parse warning is now recorded in `Base36.h` so the same false alarm doesn't recur.

## 5. Test plan

```bash
python -m py_compile vritra_gcs.py
```

1. Flash **both** boards. Both must print `schema sum 0xDDBD` — if they differ, one didn't take.
2. On the FC serial monitor confirm `// [ATT] datum: R.. P.. -> 0,0,0` at **3 s** (was 2 s — Config.h's documented value now actually applies).
3. Send `CMD_TX_ON`. Gateway rows must now have **26 comma-separated fields** before the CR.
4. Watch the GCS: SOC_PCT, GND_SPEED, AX/AY/AZ, HDOP, GNSS_SYS etc. should populate on rotation — **these were dead before this fix**. Any that stay em-dash are a genuinely unfitted sensor, not a parser fault.
5. Tilt the vehicle past vertical: the 3-D model should follow through the tumble without the ±90° flip. Numeric PITCH stays clamped to ±90° — that is correct and expected, since the panels read the atan2 estimator.

## 6. What did NOT get done

- **Neither board was compiled.** No `arduino-cli` on PATH and no ESP32/Teensy toolchain reachable from this shell; `gcc` also absent, so I verified the schema sum by parsing the real tables out of `Base36.h` and replicating the FNV-1a in Python rather than executing the C. The FC and gateway remain **unverified by a compiler** — please build both before flashing.
- `vritra_gcs.py` compiles clean (only check I could actually execute).
- Untouched from §7: the 0x57 unknown I2C device, battery cell count, and the ATT_SIGN_* bench calibration.

## What I built

[`sensor_tests/test_imu_axis_calibration/test_imu_axis_calibration.ino`](sensor_tests/test_imu_axis_calibration/test_imu_axis_calibration.ino) — standalone, touches no flight code. It copies the exact register sequence, scale factors (2048 LSB/g, 16.384 LSB/dps), `ACCEL_SCALE_CORR`, bus recovery and 100 kHz clock from `Sensors.cpp`, so what it measures is what the flight computer will see.

**⚠️ I could not open the photo you shared** — it didn't come through on my side. So the poses are defined in *words* in the sketch, and it prints the instruction before each capture. Please read each one and confirm it matches what you intended, especially which face you call FRONT.

**One deliberate design choice:** the sketch reports **absolute** angles, not datum-subtracted ones. The flight code zeroes to the boot pose; if this tool did that too, every pose would be relative to the last and the mounting would be unrecoverable. At LEVEL you should see roll ≈ 0, pitch ≈ 0 naturally — that confirms the datum without hiding the data.

## Poses, and your 10-minute holds

Seven poses: LEVEL → LEFT 90 → RIGHT 90 → FRONT 90 → BACK 90 → INVERTED → YAW +90.

It is **operator-advanced, not timed**. It averages continuously for as long as you hold, so a 10-minute pose is strictly a better measurement than a short one — press ENTER when you're done. At 50 Hz that's ~30,000 samples per pose. Live CSV streams at 10 Hz the whole time (`t_ms,pose,ax,ay,az,gx,gy,gz,amag,roll,pitch`) — capture the serial log and you have the complete raw record.

Keys: `ENTER`=capture/advance · `r`=redo pose · `v`=toggle stream · `s`=show running average · `q`=finish early.

## Two things I caught while building it

**1. A left-handed-frame bug I nearly shipped.** I first used (X forward, Y right, **Z up**) — which is *left-handed*. The determinant test would have read −1 for a perfectly good vehicle and sent you hunting a reflection that didn't exist. The right-handed aerospace body frame is Z **down**. Fixed, and verified in simulation: det = +1 for four different valid mountings, −1 only for a genuinely reflected one.

**2. `ATT_SIGN_*` cannot fix an axis swap — and that's likely your actual bug.** Simulation showed a mounting where forward lies along sensor Y: rolling the vehicle moves the *pitch* number. Three sign flips are three negations, and **no combination of negations fixes a swap**. That is exactly your "fixing one direction breaks another" symptom. The sketch now runs an axis-response check (180° vs 0° separation — completely unambiguous) and, if it fires, tells you to swap `ax`/`ay` in the attitude formula *first* and re-run, rather than handing you signs that can't work.

## What it outputs

Per-pose table (mean/σ of all six axes, |a|, flight-formula roll/pitch, gyro drift over the hold) · rotation matrix with determinant and orthogonality check · axis-response/swap verdict · recommended `ATT_SIGN_ROLL/PITCH/YAW` as paste-ready `#define` lines · six-position accel bias & scale per axis · and `GYRO_BIAS_*_DPS` from the LEVEL hold, with uncorrected yaw drift in deg/min — the 10-minute hold makes that number genuinely good.

## Not done

**The sketch is not compiled** — still no `arduino-cli` or Teensy toolchain reachable from this shell. I verified balance, definition order, and simulated the frame/sign/swap math in Python, but a compiler has not seen it. Build it in the Arduino IDE before the session; if it throws anything, paste the error and I'll fix it immediately.

Send me the report output when you've run it and I'll apply the results to `Config.h`.

## The answer: your board is mounted rotated 90° about Z

**sensor +X → vehicle RIGHT · sensor +Y → vehicle FRONT · sensor +Z → UP**

I read the plateaus out of your raw stream, not the printed means:

| Pose | ax | ay | az | dominant |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LEVEL | −0.029 | −0.052 | **+0.997** | +Z |
| LEFT 90 | **+1.005** | −0.161 | +0.029 | **+X** |
| RIGHT 90 | **−1.002** | −0.216 | −0.019 | **−X** |
| FRONT 90 | −0.192 | **−1.012** | −0.021 | **−Y** |
| BACK 90 | +0.135 | **+0.984** | −0.028 | **+Y** |
| INVERTED | −0.029 | −0.064 | **−1.047** | −Z |

Rolling drives **X**, pitching drives **Y**. The cross-check agrees independently: rolling moved *pitch* by 133.7° and only *roll* by 13.2°.

**This is why months of sign-flipping never worked.** A swap is not a negation — no combination of three `ATT_SIGN_*` constants can express a permutation, so fixing roll always broke pitch. Your `Config.h` actually predicted this failure mode in a comment; nobody had run the test to confirm it. I've kept that paragraph and marked it resolved.

## What changed

`Sensors.cpp` — swap where it belongs:
```c
rollRaw  = atan2f(-ax, az);
pitchRaw = atan2f( ay, sqrtf(ax*ax + az*az));
```
Verified against all six poses: RIGHT +91.1, LEFT −88.4, BACK +82.0, FRONT −79.2, LEVEL +1.7/−3.0, INVERTED +178.4. Every one correct.

`Config.h` — **ROLL +1, PITCH +1, YAW −1**. Roll and pitch need no negation once the swap is right. Yaw is −1 because +Z points up, so clockwise-from-above is negative under the right-hand rule — your log confirms sustained negative `gz` through the turn.

Quaternion fed in vehicle axes `(ay, ax, −az)`, determinant **+1**. The naive `(ay, ax, +az)` is a reflection and would integrate rotations backwards. This one is **maths-checked but not bench-verified** — confirm the 3-D model tilts correctly before CDR.

## Two things I did not accept from the report

**The printed summary was invalid.** Your home→pose→home procedure meant the sketch averaged the level dwell, both transitions and the pose together — hence |a| = 0.79–0.88, determinant 0.76, and scale factors of 0.70/0.79. All artefacts. I've added a prominent warning to the sketch: **press ENTER while still holding the pose.**

**Don't paste the gyro bias.** The LEVEL hold was 8 s, not 10 min, and it was disturbed — σ of 1.2–2.6 dps against ~0.1 expected, with visible table-knock spikes (+11.4, −7.3 dps). The `gz` discrepancy is worth ~9°/min of yaw drift, so it matters. Re-run pose 1 alone, solid surface, untouched, ≥10 min; σ should drop below ~0.3 dps.

Also noted: accel Z has a −0.025 g bias (reads +0.997 up, −1.047 inverted) — documented, no action, a single scalar can't fix a per-axis asymmetry. And the −387° yaw magnitude is untrustworthy because that capture began while still inverted; the *sign* is fine.

    def compute(self, d: dict, packet_count: int = 0, present=None):
        present = present if present is not None else set(ALL_FIELDS)
        r, dp = {}, set()

        def have(*keys):
            return all(k in present for k in keys)

        try:
            t_s  = _finite(d.get("TIME_S"))
            alt  = _finite(d.get("ALT_M"))
            pres = _finite(d.get("PRES_PA"))
            temp = _finite(d.get("TEMP_C"))
            hum  = _finite(d.get("HUM_PCT"))

            # 1. Extract Raw Variables
            if have("ACC_X", "ACC_Y", "ACC_Z"):
                ax = _finite(d.get("ACC_X")) / G_EARTH
                ay = _finite(d.get("ACC_Y")) / G_EARTH
                az = _finite(d.get("ACC_Z")) / G_EARTH
                accel_ok = True
            else:
                ax = _finite(d.get("AX"))
                ay = _finite(d.get("AY"))
                az = _finite(d.get("AZ"))
                accel_ok = have("AX", "AY", "AZ")

            spin_r = _finite(d.get("SPIN_RATE"))
            gx, gy = _finite(d.get("GX")), _finite(d.get("GY"))
            gz = _finite(d.get("GZ")) or spin_r
            mx, my, mz = (_finite(d.get("MX")), _finite(d.get("MY")),
                          _finite(d.get("MZ")))

            mag_ok = have("MX", "MY", "MZ")
            bus_v, bus_i = _finite(d.get("BUS_V")), _finite(d.get("BUS_I_MA"))

            # ---- vertical speed --------------------------------------
            if (self.t_last is not None and t_s > self.t_last
                    and have("ALT_M", "TIME_S")):
                dt = t_s - self.t_last
                vsp = (alt - self.alt_last) / dt if self.alt_last is not None else 0.0
                vsp_ok = True
            else:
                dt, vsp, vsp_ok = 0.0, 0.0, False
            r["VSPEED"], r["DESCENT"] = vsp, (-vsp if vsp < 0 else 0.0)
            if vsp_ok:
                dp.update(["VSPEED", "DESCENT"])

            # ---- ABSOLUTE EULER MATH FIX -----------------------------
            # Bypasses the flight computer's cross-coupled filter.
            # Physical Board: X=Right, Y=Forward, Z=Up
            roll = math.degrees(math.atan2(-ax, az))
            pitch = math.degrees(math.atan2(ay, math.sqrt(ax * ax + az * az)))
            
            r["ROLL"], r["PITCH"] = roll, pitch
            a_mag = math.sqrt(ax * ax + ay * ay + az * az) or 1.0
            r["TILT"] = math.degrees(math.acos(max(-1.0, min(1.0, az / a_mag))))
            
            if accel_ok:
                dp.update(["ROLL", "PITCH", "TILT"])

            # ---- tilt-compensated heading ----------------------------
            # Map physical (Right, Forward, Up) to NED (Forward, Right, Down)
            mx_ned, my_ned, mz_ned = my, mx, -mz
            cr, sr = math.cos(math.radians(roll)), math.sin(math.radians(roll))
            cp, sp = math.cos(math.radians(pitch)), math.sin(math.radians(pitch))
            
            mxh = mx_ned * cp + mz_ned * sp
            myh = mx_ned * sr * sp + my_ned * cr - mz_ned * sr * cp
            hdg = (math.degrees(math.atan2(-myh, mxh)) + 360.0) % 360.0
            r["HEADING"] = hdg
            r["YAW"] = hdg
            
            if mag_ok and accel_ok:
                dp.update(["HEADING", "YAW"])

            # ---- spin / stability -------------------------------------
            spin_rate = spin_r if "SPIN_RATE" in present else gz
            spin_ok = ("SPIN_RATE" in present) or ("GZ" in present)
            omega_t = math.sqrt(gx * gx + gy * gy)
            instab = min(1.0, omega_t / 90.0)
            r["OMEGA_T"], r["SPIN_DPS"] = omega_t, spin_rate
            r["INSTAB"], r["STABLE"] = instab, 1.0 - instab
            r["H_BODY"] = I_T * math.radians(omega_t) * 1000.0
            r["NUTATION"] = math.degrees(
                abs(I_Z - I_T) / I_T * math.radians(abs(spin_rate)))
            r["CONING"] = omega_t
            if have("GX", "GY"):
                dp.update(["OMEGA_T", "INSTAB", "STABLE", "H_BODY", "CONING"])
            if spin_ok:
                dp.update(["SPIN_DPS", "NUTATION"])

            # ---- atmosphere ------------------------------------------
            rho = self.isa_density(pres, temp)
            r["AIR_RHO"] = rho
            r["P_ALT"] = self.isa_pressure_alt(pres)
            r["DENS_ALT"] = self.density_alt(pres, temp)
            r["DEW_POINT"] = self.dew_point(temp, hum)
            r["CLOUD_BASE"] = self.cloud_base_agl(temp, r["DEW_POINT"])
            if have("PRES_PA", "TEMP_C"):
                dp.update(["AIR_RHO", "P_ALT", "DENS_ALT"])
            if have("TEMP_C", "HUM_PCT"):
                dp.update(["DEW_POINT", "CLOUD_BASE"])

            # ---- speed / aerodynamics --------------------------------
            gnd = _finite(d.get("GND_SPEED"))
            v3d = math.sqrt(gnd * gnd + vsp * vsp)
            r["SPEED_3D"] = v3d
            self.peak_speed = max(self.peak_speed, v3d)
            r["PEAK_V"] = self.peak_speed
            speed_ok = ("GND_SPEED" in present) or vsp_ok
            if speed_ok:
                dp.update(["SPEED_3D", "PEAK_V"])

            sos = (math.sqrt(GAMMA_AIR * R_AIR * (temp + 273.15))
                   if temp > -273 else 340.0)
            r["DYN_Q"] = 0.5 * rho * v3d * v3d
            r["MACH"] = v3d / sos if sos > 0 else 0.0
            r["REYNOLDS"] = (rho * v3d * CANSAT_LEN_M) / MU_AIR_REF
            r["SOS"] = sos
            if speed_ok and have("PRES_PA", "TEMP_C"):
                dp.update(["DYN_Q", "MACH", "REYNOLDS", "SOS"])

            sink = max(0.0, -vsp)
            r["SINK_RATE"] = sink
            r["GLIDE_LD"] = (gnd / sink) if sink > 0.1 else 0.0
            r["GLIDE_SLOPE"] = (-math.degrees(math.atan2(sink, max(gnd, 0.01)))
                                if sink > 0.1 else 0.0)
            if vsp_ok:
                dp.add("SINK_RATE")
                if "GND_SPEED" in present:
                    dp.update(["GLIDE_LD", "GLIDE_SLOPE"])

            # ---- power ------------------------------------------------
            srv_v, srv_i = _finite(d.get("SERVO_V")), _finite(d.get("SERVO_I_MA"))
            p_bus, p_srv = bus_v * bus_i / 1000.0, srv_v * srv_i / 1000.0
            r["P_BUS"], r["P_SERVO"], r["P_TOTAL"] = p_bus, p_srv, p_bus + p_srv
            if 0 < dt < 5.0 and have("BUS_V", "BUS_I_MA"):
                self.energy_wh += (p_bus + p_srv) * dt / 3600.0
            r["ENERGY_WH"] = self.energy_wh
            if have("BUS_V", "BUS_I_MA"):
                dp.add("P_BUS")
            if have("SERVO_V", "SERVO_I_MA"):
                dp.add("P_SERVO")
            if have("BUS_V", "BUS_I_MA", "SERVO_V", "SERVO_I_MA"):
                dp.update(["P_TOTAL", "ENERGY_WH"])

            # ---- link -------------------------------------------------
            rssi = _finite(d.get("RSSI"))
            r["LINK_MARGIN"] = (rssi - LORA_SENS_DBM) if rssi < 0 else 0.0
            if "RSSI" in present:
                dp.add("LINK_MARGIN")

            # ---- high-g -----------------------------------------------
            ahx, ahy, ahz = (_finite(d.get("AHX")), _finite(d.get("AHY")),
                             _finite(d.get("AHZ")))
            g_hi = math.sqrt(ahx * ahx + ahy * ahy + ahz * ahz)
            if have("AHX", "AHY", "AHZ"):
                self.peak_g_hi = max(self.peak_g_hi, g_hi)
                dp.update(["G_HI", "G_HI_PK"])
            r["G_HI"], r["G_HI_PK"] = g_hi, self.peak_g_hi

            # ---- air quality / spectral -------------------------------
            r["AQI_PM"] = self.aqi_from_pm25(_finite(d.get("PM25")))
            if "PM25" in present:
                dp.add("AQI_PM")
            spec = [k for k in ALL_FIELDS if k.startswith("S_") and k != "S_FLK"]
            r["IRR_TOT"] = sum(_finite(d.get(k)) for k in spec)
            if any(k in present for k in spec):
                dp.add("IRR_TOT")

            # ---- navigation -------------------------------------------
            lat, lon = _finite(d.get("LAT")), _finite(d.get("LON"))
            gnss_ok = have("LAT", "LON")
            if gnss_ok and (lat or lon) and self.lat0 is None:
                self.lat0, self.lon0 = lat, lon
            if self.lat0 is not None and gnss_ok and (lat or lon):
                r["DIST_M"] = self.hav_dist(self.lat0, self.lon0, lat, lon)
                r["BEARING"] = self.bearing(self.lat0, self.lon0, lat, lon)
                dp.update(["DIST_M", "BEARING"])
            else:
                r["DIST_M"] = r["BEARING"] = 0.0

            r["BARO_GPS_DALT"] = abs(alt - _finite(d.get("GPS_ALT_M")))
            if have("ALT_M", "GPS_ALT_M"):
                dp.add("BARO_GPS_DALT")

            # ---- apogee / AGL -----------------------------------------
            if "ALT_M" in present:
                self.apogee_m = max(self.apogee_m, alt)
                if self.takeoff_alt is None and alt > 5:
                    self.takeoff_alt = alt
                dp.update(["APOGEE_M", "AGL_M"])
            r["APOGEE_M"] = self.apogee_m
            r["AGL_M"] = alt - (self.takeoff_alt or 0.0)

            r["TTL_S"] = (max(alt, 0.0) / sink) if sink > 0.1 else 0.0
            if vsp_ok and "ALT_M" in present:
                dp.add("TTL_S")

            if "LGT_FLAG" in present and _finite(d.get("LGT_FLAG")) > 0:
                self.lightning_log.append(
                    {"t": t_s, "dist_km": _finite(d.get("LGT_DIST")),
                     "intensity": _finite(d.get("LGT_INT"))})

            # ---- storage estimate --------------------------------------
            est = packet_count * BYTES_PER_PACKET
            if "SD_PCT" not in present:
                r["SD_PCT"] = min(100.0, 100.0 * est / SD_CAP_BYTES)
            if "FLASH_PCT" not in present:
                r["FLASH_PCT"] = min(100.0, 100.0 * est / FLASH_CAP_BYTES)

            self.t_last, self.alt_last = t_s, alt
        except Exception:
            log.exception("Physics.compute non-fatal")

        for k, v in list(r.items()):
            if isinstance(v, float) and not math.isfinite(v):
                r[k] = 0.0
        return r, dp

    def _update_instruments(self):
        att_ok = {"ROLL", "PITCH"} <= self.dp or self.limits.params["ROLL"].ever_seen
        roll = _finite(self.dv.get("ROLL"), 0.0)
        pitch = _finite(self.dv.get("PITCH"), 0.0)
        yaw = _finite(self.dv.get("YAW"), 0.0)
        spin = _finite(self.dv.get("SPIN_DPS"), 0.0)

        self.adi.set_attitude(roll, pitch, att_ok)
        hdg_ok = "HEADING" in self.dp or self.limits.params["HEADING"].ever_seen
        self.compass.set_heading(yaw, hdg_ok)
        
        if att_ok:
            # The original 3D geometry engine is defined as X=Right, Y=Forward.
            # In its matrix, 'arg 1' rotates about X (causes Nose Pitch).
            # 'arg 2' rotates about Y (causes Left/Right Roll).
            # Passing (pitch, roll) seamlessly aligns our math to the render geometry.
            self.cansat.set_attitude(pitch, roll, yaw, spin, True)
            
            # CRITICAL SEVER: Force the quaternion off.
            # The telemetry QUAT_X/Y/Z is mathematically poisoned by the left-handed 
            # physical board frame running through the flight computer's filter.
            # Setting this to None forces the 3D model to fall back to the clean 
            # Euler angles we just mapped above.
            self.cansat.set_quaternion(None)

here is what i did properly add it to document 

1. The Root Cause: Cross-Coupled Hardware Filter
Inside the CanSat, the flight computer runs a sensor fusion algorithm (likely a Mahony or Madgwick filter) to calculate Euler angles (`ATT_ROLL`, `ATT_PITCH`) and quaternions (`QUAT_X/Y/Z`). These algorithms strictly require the IMU data to be fed into them using a standard aerospace coordinate frame (such as North-East-Down or Forward-Right-Down).
Because your IMU breakout board was mounted differently than the filter expected, the raw X, Y, and Z axes were fed into the mathematical filter incorrectly. When you rolled the CanSat, the filter interpreted the gravity vector shifting across its axes as a complex diagonal movement. This mathematically "cross-coupled" the axes, meaning a pure physical Roll was corrupted into a mixture of Roll and Pitch (as seen in your logs where a 90° roll yielded 68° of pitch).
2. The Physics Engine Override (The Math Fix)
To fix this without reprogramming the flight computer, the GCS software was modified to completely ignore the poisoned fusion data (`ATT_ROLL`, `ATT_PITCH`, and `QUAT`) coming from the telemetry stream.
Instead, the `compute` function now intercepts the raw, uncorrupted accelerometer gravity vectors (`ACC_X`, `ACC_Y`, `ACC_Z`). It bypasses the flight computer's fusion filter entirely and calculates the absolute attitude natively on the ground station using standard trigonometric formulas:
$\text{Roll} = \arctan2(-A_x, A_z)$
$\text{Pitch} = \arctan2(A_y, \sqrt{A_x^2 + A_z^2})$
This ensures the Engineering panels and CSV logs record the true, gravity-anchored physical orientation of the CanSat, completely immune to the flight computer's internal mathematical errors.
3. The 3D Render Engine Correction (The Visual Fix)
The `CanSatView` widget uses an internal projection matrix to draw the 3D cylinder. By default, its internal geometry matrix expects the first argument to rotate the model around the lateral axis (Pitch) and the second argument to rotate it around the longitudinal axis (Roll).
The original code was passing `(roll, pitch)`, which effectively mapped the aircraft's Roll data to the 3D model's Pitch axis, and vice versa.
The fix in `_update_instruments` does two things:

1. Argument Swap: It passes `(pitch, roll)` to the renderer, seamlessly mapping the clean GCS physics calculations to the correct visual geometry axes.
2. Quaternion Severing: It explicitly executes `self.cansat.set_quaternion(None)`. This acts as a software kill-switch, blocking the corrupted hardware quaternion from reaching the renderer and forcing the 3D model to fall back to the newly cleaned Euler angles. 
3. and aso verify it
